Several of the children from each school in the township were to speak pieces in the Town Hall. Betsy was to recite *Barbara Frietchie*, her first love in that school, but she droned it over with none of her usual pleasure, her eyes on little ’Lias’s smiling face, so unconscious of its dinginess.

===Chinese===
小姑娘们退到她们的石头堆上，痛心地商量着这次失败。拉尔夫和其他男孩就在旁边，正埋头玩弹子。莱亚斯则得意洋洋地进教室，向本顿小姐展示自己去了。

明天就是阵亡将士纪念日（Decoration Day），学校花了不少时间排练孩子们要在村里纪念活动上表演的朗诵。全镇各所学校都要选送几个孩子到镇公所去朗诵。轮到贝西朗诵《芭芭拉·弗里奇》（Barbara Frietchie）——那是她来这所学校后最先爱上的东西——可她念得有气无力，全无平日的兴味，眼睛只顾盯着莱亚斯那张笑眯眯的小脸，那张脸对自己的灰头土脸浑然不觉。

===Original===
At noon time the boys disappeared down toward the swimming-hole. They often took a swim at noon and nobody thought anything about it on that day. The little girls ate their lunch on their rock, mourning over the failure of their plans, and scheming ways to meet the new obstacle. Stashie suggested, “Couldn’t your Aunt Abigail invite him up to your house for supper and then give him a bath afterward?” But Betsy, although she had never heard of treating a supper-guest in this way, was sure that it was not possible. She shook her head sadly, her eyes on the far-off gleam of white where the boys jumped up and down in their swimming-hole. That was not a good name for it, because there was only one part of it deep enough to swim in. Mostly it was a shallow bay in an arm of the river, where the water was only up to a little boy’s knees and where there was almost no current. The sun beating down on it made it quite warm, and even the first-graders’ mothers allowed them to go in. They only jumped up and down and squealed and splashed each other, but they enjoyed that quite as much as Frank and Harry, the two seventh-graders, enjoyed their swooping dives from the springboard over the pool. They were late in getting back from the river that day and Miss Benton had to ring her bell hard in that direction before they came trooping up and clattered into the schoolroom, where the girls already sat, their eyes lowered virtuously to their books, with a prim air of self-righteousness. *They* were never late!

===Chinese===
中午，男孩子们一哄而下，往游泳洞那边去了。他们常在中午游上一泳，那天也没人在意。小姑娘们坐在石头上吃午饭，一面为计划的失败哀叹，一面琢磨着怎么对付这个新难题。斯塔希提议：「能不能让你阿比盖尔姨妈请他上你家吃晚饭，饭后给他洗个澡？」贝西虽然从没听说过这样招待晚饭客人，却断定这行不通。她难过地摇摇头，眼睛望着远处那片闪动的白光——男孩们正在游泳洞里蹦来蹦去。其实那地方叫这个名字并不合适，因为只有一处水深得能游泳。它多半是河汊里的一片浅水湾，水深只到小男孩的膝盖，几乎没什么水流。太阳直晒着，水相当暖和，连一年级学生的妈妈们都容许孩子下水。他们只是在水里蹦跳、尖叫、互相泼水，可他们那份快活，丝毫不亚于弗兰克和哈里——两个七年级学生——从池边跳板上俯冲入水的乐趣。那天他们从河边回来晚了，本顿小姐只好朝那个方向使劲摇铃，他们才成群结队地跑上坡来，噼里啪啦涌进教室。姑娘们早已坐好，一双双眼睛规规矩矩地垂在课本上，一脸端庄的正气。她们可从不迟到！

===Original===
Betsy was reciting her arithmetic. She was getting on famously with that. Weeks ago, as soon as Miss Benton had seen the confusion of the little girl’s mind, the two had settled down to a serious struggle with that subject. Miss Benton had had Betsy recite all by herself, so she wouldn’t be flurried by the others; and to begin with had gone back, back, back to bedrock, to things Betsy absolutely knew, to the 2 × 2’s and the 3 × 3’s. And then, very cautiously, a step at a time, they had advanced, stopping short whenever Betsy felt a beginning of that bewildered “guessing” impulse which made her answer wildly at random.

===Chinese===
此时贝西正在背算术。这门功课她进展得非常顺利。几个星期前，本顿小姐一眼看出小姑娘脑子里的那团乱麻，两人便沉下心来，跟这门功课认真较上了劲。本顿小姐让贝西单独一个人背，免得她被别人搅得心慌；起初一退到底，退到基石上，退到贝西绝对有把握的东西上——2 × 2 和 3 × 3。然后，非常小心地，一步一挪地往前推进，只要贝西觉出那股令人发懵的「瞎猜」冲动又要冒头——就是害她不管三七二十一胡乱报个答案的那股冲动——就立刻停住。

===Original===
After a while, in the dark night which arithmetic had always been to her, Betsy began to make out a few definite outlines, which were always there, facts which she knew to be so without guessing from the expression of her teacher’s face. From that moment her progress had been rapid, one sure fact hooking itself on to another, and another one on to that. She attacked a page of problems now with a zest and self-confidence which made her arithmetic lessons among the most interesting hours at school. On that day she was standing up at the board, a piece of chalk in her hand, chewing her tongue and thinking hard how to find out the amount of wallpaper needed for a room 12 feet square with two doors and two windows in it, when her eye fell on little ’Lias, bent over his reading book. She forgot her arithmetic, she forgot where she was. She stared and stared, till Ellen, catching the direction of her eyes, looked and stared too. Little ’Lias was *clean*, preternaturally, almost wetly clean. His face was clean and shining, his ears shone pink and fair, his hands were absolutely spotless, even his hay-colored hair was clean and, still damp, brushed flatly back till it shone in the sun. Betsy blinked her eyes a great many times, thinking she must be dreaming, but every time she opened them there was ’Lias, looking white and polished like a new willow whistle.

===Chinese===
过了一阵子，在算术这片向来漆黑的夜里，贝西开始辨认出几个固定的轮廓——它们一直都在那儿——这些事实她确信如此，用不着再去揣摩老师脸上的表情。从那一刻起，她进步飞快，一个确凿的事实勾住另一个，又一个再勾上来。如今她做一整页习题，劲头十足、信心满满，算术课成了她在学校里最有意思的时光之一。那天她正站在黑板前，手里捏着一截粉笔，咬着舌头苦苦思索：一间 12 英尺见方的屋子，有两扇门两扇窗，要算出糊墙纸需要多少。就在这时，她的目光落在正埋头看识字课本的小莱亚斯身上。她忘了算术，忘了自己身在何处。她看了又看，直看到埃伦顺着她的视线望过去，也看直了眼。小莱亚斯是干净的，干净得出奇，简直是水淋淋的干净。他的脸干净发亮，耳朵粉红洁净地闪着光，两只手一丝污渍也没有，连他那草灰色的头发也干干净净，还带着潮气，平平地梳向脑后，在太阳底下闪闪发亮。贝西眨了好多次眼睛，心想自己准是在做梦，可每次睁开眼，莱亚斯都在那儿，白净光滑得像一只崭新的柳木哨。

===Original===
Somebody poked her hard in the ribs. She started and, turning, saw Ralph, who was doing a sum beside her on the board, scowling at her under his black brows. “Quit gawking at ’Lias,” he said under his breath. “You make me tired!” Something conscious and shamefaced in his manner made Betsy understand at once what had happened. Ralph had taken ’Lias down to the little boys’ wading-place and had washed him all over. She remembered now that they had a piece of yellow soap there.

Her face broke into a radiant smile and she began to say something to Ralph about how nice that was of him, but he frowned again and said, crossly, “Aw, cut it out! Look at what you’ve done there! If I couldn’t 9 × 8 and get it right!”

===Chinese===
有人在她的肋部狠狠捅了一下。她一惊，回过头，看见拉尔夫正在她旁边的黑板上做一道题，黑眉毛底下皱着眉头瞪她。「别死盯着莱亚斯看了，」他压低声音说，「你看得我烦死了！」他神情里那点心虚和害臊，让贝西立刻明白了是怎么回事。是拉尔夫把莱亚斯带到小男孩们蹚水的地方，从头到脚给他洗了个干净。她这才想起来，他们那儿有一块黄肥皂。

她脸上绽开一个灿烂的笑容，正想对拉尔夫说一声他这样真好，他却又皱起眉头，没好气地说：「去去去，别说了！看看你自己写的！要是我连 9 × 8 都算不对，还像话吗！」

===Original===
“How queer boys are!” thought Betsy, erasing her mistake and putting down the right answer. But she did not try to speak to Ralph again about ’Lias, not even after school, when she saw ’Lias going home with a new cap on his head which she recognized as Ralph’s. She just looked at Ralph’s bare head, and smiled her eyes at him, keeping the rest of her face sober, the way Cousin Ann did. For just a minute Ralph almost smiled back. At least he looked quite friendly. They stepped along toward home together, the first time Ralph had ever condescended to walk beside a girl.

===Chinese===
「男孩子可真怪！」贝西一面想，一面擦掉错处，写上正确答案。此后她没再跟拉尔夫提莱亚斯的事，放学后也没有——虽然她看见莱亚斯头上戴了顶新便帽，认出那正是拉尔夫的。她只是瞧了瞧拉尔夫光着的脑袋，用眼睛朝他一笑，脸上其余部分端着不动，就像安表姨那样。有那么一小会儿，拉尔夫几乎也要回她一个笑。至少他看上去相当友善。他们并肩朝家走去——这可是拉尔夫头一回肯屈尊走在女孩子旁边。

===Original===
“We got a new colt,” he said.

“Have you?” she said. “What color?”

“Black, with a white star, and they’re going to let me ride him when he’s old enough.”

“My! Won’t that be nice!” said Betsy.

===Chinese===
「我们家添了匹小马驹，」他说。

「是吗？」她说，「什么颜色的？」

「黑的，脑门上有颗白星，等它再长大点儿，他们就让骑了。」

「哎呀！那多好呀！」贝西说。

===Original===
And all the time they were both thinking of little ’Lias with his new clothes and his sweet, thin face shining with cleanliness.

“Do you like spruce gum?” asked Ralph.

“Oh, *I love* gum!” said Betsy.

“Well, I’ll bring you down a chunk tomorrow, if I don’t forget it,” said Ralph, turning off at the crossroads.

They had not mentioned ’Lias at all.

===Chinese===
嘴上说着这些，他们俩心里却一直想着小莱亚斯，想着他的新衣裳，和他那张洗得干干净净、发着亮的清瘦可爱的小脸。

「你爱吃云杉树胶吗？」拉尔夫问。

「哦，我太爱吃树胶了！」贝西说。

「那好，明天我给你带一大块来，只要我别忘了。」拉尔夫说着，在岔路口拐下了大路。

自始至终，他们一个字也没提莱亚斯。

===Original===
The next day they were to have school only in the morning. In the afternoon they were to go in a big hay-wagon down to the village to the “exercises.” ’Lias came to school in his new blue-serge trousers and his white blouse. The little girls gloated over his appearance, and hung around him, for who was to “visit school” that morning but Mr. Pond himself! Cousin Ann had arranged it somehow. It took Cousin Ann to fix things! During recess, as they were playing still-pond-no-more-moving on the playground, Mr. Pond and Uncle Henry drew up to the edge of the playground, stopped their horse, and, talking and laughing together, watched the children at play. Betsy looked hard at the big, burly, kind-faced man with the smiling eyes and the hearty laugh, and decided that he would “do” perfectly for ’Lias. But what she decided was to have little importance, apparently, for after all he would not get out of the wagon, but said he’d have to drive right on to the village. Just like that, with no excuse other than a careless glance at his watch. No, he guessed he wouldn’t have time, this morning, he said. Betsy cast an imploring look up into Uncle Henry’s face, but evidently he felt himself quite helpless, too. Oh, if only Cousin Ann had come! *She* would have marched him into the schoolhouse double-quick. But Uncle Henry was not Cousin Ann, and though Betsy saw him, as they drove away, conscientiously point out little ’Lias, resplendent and shining, Mr. Pond only nodded absently, as though, he were thinking of something else.

===Chinese===
第二天只上半天课。下午，大家要坐一辆大干草车下村里去参加「纪念活动」。莱亚斯穿着他那条新的蓝哔叽裤子和白罩衫来上学。小姑娘们看着他这副模样心花怒放，围着他团团转——因为那天上午要来「参观学校」的，不是别人，正是庞德先生本人！这是安表姨不知怎么张罗成的。这种事就得安表姨出面才办得成！课间休息时，孩子们正在操场上玩「静水池——不许再动」，庞德先生和亨利姨父的车在操场边上停了下来，两人勒住马，说说笑笑，看孩子们玩耍。贝西打量着这个高大壮实、面容和善的男人——笑眯眯的眼睛，爽朗的笑声——认定他给莱亚斯「当爹」再合适不过。可她的认定显然没多大用处，因为他终究不肯下车，只说他还得赶着直接进村去。说走就走，除了心不在焉地看一眼手表，连个像样的理由都没有。不，他想他今天上午没那个工夫，他说。贝西仰起脸，用恳求的目光望着亨利姨父，可他显然也觉得自己无能为力。唉，要是安表姨来了该多好！她准会押着他一路小跑进校舍。可亨利姨父不是安表姨。虽然贝西看见他们驱车离开时，他还特意指了指容光焕发、鲜亮夺目的小莱亚斯，庞德先生却只是心不在焉地点点头，好像心里想着别的事。

===Original===
Betsy could have cried with disappointment; but she and the other girls, putting their heads together for comfort, told each other that there was time enough yet. Mr. Pond would not leave town till tomorrow. Perhaps﻿ ﻿… there was still some hope.

===Chinese===
贝西失望得几乎要哭出来；不过她和姑娘们凑在一起互相宽慰，说时间还来得及。庞德先生明天才会离镇。也许……还有一线希望。

===Original===
But that afternoon even this last hope was dashed. As they gathered at the schoolhouse, the girls fresh and crisp in their newly starched dresses, with red or blue hair-ribbons, the boys very self-conscious in their dark suits, clean collars, new caps (all but Ralph), and blacked shoes, there was no little ’Lias. They waited and waited, but there was no sign of him. Finally Uncle Henry, who was to drive the straw-ride down to town, looked at his watch, gathered up the reins, and said they would be late if they didn’t start right away. Maybe ’Lias had had a chance to ride in with somebody else.

===Chinese===
可那天下午，连这最后一线希望也破灭了。大家在校舍前集合，姑娘们穿着浆得挺括的新连衣裙，系着红色或蓝色的发带，个个清爽鲜灵；男孩子们穿着深色套装、干净的领子，戴着新便帽（唯独拉尔夫没有），皮鞋擦得乌亮，一个个浑身不自在——偏偏不见小莱亚斯。他们等了又等，仍不见他的人影。最后，负责赶干草车送大家进镇的亨利姨父看了看表，抓起缰绳，说再不马上动身就要迟到了。也许莱亚斯碰上了别人，捎他进镇去了。

===Original===
They all piled in, the horses stepped off, the wheels grated on the stones. And just at that moment a dismal sound of sobbing wails reached them from the woodshed back of the schoolhouse. The children tumbled out as fast as they had tumbled in, and ran back, Betsy and Ralph at their head. There in the woodshed was little ’Lias, huddled in the corner behind some wood, crying and crying and crying, digging his fists into his eyes, his face all smeared with tears and dirt. And he was dressed again in his filthy, torn old overalls and ragged shirt. His poor little bare feet shone with a piteous cleanliness in that dark place.

===Chinese===
大家一拥而上爬进车，马迈开步子，车轮碾过石子咯咯作响。就在这时，一阵呜呜咽咽的哭声从校舍后面的柴棚里传来。孩子们又像刚才挤进去时那样飞快地翻下车，朝柴棚跑去，贝西和拉尔夫冲在最前头。柴棚里，小莱亚斯缩在柴垛后面的墙角，哭啊，哭啊，哭个不停，两个拳头死命往眼睛里抠，脸上涂满了眼泪和泥污。他又穿上了那身又脏又破的旧工装裤和破烂衬衫。他那双可怜的小光脚在昏暗的地方闪着光，干净得让人心酸。

===Original===
“What’s the matter? What’s the matter?” the children asked him all at once. He flung himself on Ralph, burying his face in the other boy’s coat, and sobbed out some disjointed story which only Ralph could hear﻿ ﻿… and then as last and final climax of the disaster, who should come looking over the shoulders of the children but Uncle Henry *and* Mr. Pond! And ’Lias all ragged and dirty again! Betsy sat down weakly on a pile of wood, utterly disheartened. What was the use of anything!

===Chinese===
「怎么了？到底怎么了？」孩子们七嘴八舌地问他。他一头扑到拉尔夫身上，把脸埋在对方的衣襟里，抽抽噎噎地讲了一通话，只有拉尔夫听见了……接着，作为这场灾难最后最狠的一击，越过孩子们的肩头探看进来的，竟不是别人，正是亨利姨父和庞德先生！而莱亚斯偏偏又是这副破衣烂衫、满身泥污的样子！贝西浑身发软，在一堆木柴上坐了下去，彻底灰了心。什么都白搭了，干什么都没用了！

===Original===
Ralph turned, with an angry toss of his dark head, and told them bitterly, over the heads of the children: “He just had some decent clothes.﻿ ﻿… First ones he’s *ever* had! And he was plotting on going to the exercises in the Town Hall. And that darned old skunk of a stepfather has gone and taken ’em and sold ’em to get whiskey. I’d like to *kill* him!”

===Chinese===
拉尔夫猛地一甩黑脑袋转过身来，越过孩子们的头顶，狠狠地说：「他好不容易才有了一身体面衣裳。……那可是他有生以来头一身！他一直盘算着穿去镇公所参加活动。可那个该死的老混蛋继父，竟把衣裳拿去卖掉换威士忌了。我真想宰了他！」

===Original===
Betsy could have flung her arms around Ralph, he looked so exactly the way she felt. “Yes, he is a darned old skunk!” she said to herself, rejoicing in the bad words she did not know before. It *took* bad words to qualify what had happened.

===Chinese===
贝西真想张开双臂搂住拉尔夫，他那副神情，跟她心里的感受一模一样。「没错，他就是个该死的老混蛋！」她对自己说，一面为这句从前不会骂的粗话暗自痛快。只有粗话，才配得上眼下这桩事。

===Original===
She saw an electric spark pass from Ralph’s blazing eyes to Mr. Pond’s broad face, now grim and fierce. She saw Mr. Pond step forward, brushing the children out of his way, like a giant among dwarfs. She saw him stoop and pick little ’Lias up in his great, strong arms, and, holding him close, stride furiously out of the woodshed, across the playground to the buggy which was waiting for him.

===Chinese===
她看见一道电光，从拉尔夫冒火的眼睛里射到庞德先生宽阔的脸上——那张脸此刻又冷峻又凶悍。她看见庞德先生大步上前，把孩子们拨到一边，像巨人走在矮人堆里。她看见他弯下腰，用那双强壮的大胳膊抱起小莱亚斯，紧紧搂在怀里，怒气冲冲地大步走出柴棚，穿过操场，朝那辆等着他的单马马车走去。

===Original===
“He’ll go to the exercises all right!” he called back over his shoulder in a great roar. “He’ll go, if I have to buy out the whole town to get him an outfit! And that whelp won’t get these clothes, either; you hear me say so!”

===Chinese===
「他准能去参加活动！」他回过头来大声吼道，「他就是去定了，哪怕我把整个镇子的铺子买空，也要给他置办一身行头！那身衣裳，那头小畜生也别想再弄到手；你们都听见了，这话是我说的！」

===Original===
He sprang into the buggy and, holding ’Lias on his lap, took up the reins and drove rapidly forward.

===Chinese===
他跳上马车，把莱亚斯放在膝上，抓起缰绳，飞快地驾车去了。

===Original===
They saw little ’Lias again, entering the Town Hall, holding fast to Mr. Pond’s hand. He was magnificent in a whole suit of store clothes, coat and all, and he wore white stockings and neat, low shoes, like a city child!

===Chinese===
后来，他们又见到了小莱亚斯：他紧紧攥着庞德先生的手，正走进镇公所。他穿着一整套从铺子里买来的成衣，连上衣都有，神气极了，脚上配着白长袜和一双干净的低帮鞋，活像个城里的孩子！

===Original===
They saw him later, up on the platform, squeaking out his little patriotic poem, his eyes, shining like stars, fixed on one broad, smiling face in the audience. When he finished he was overcome with shyness by the applause, and for a moment forgot to turn and leave the platform. He hung his head, and, looking out from under his eyebrows, gave a quaint, shy little smile at the audience. Betsy saw Mr. Pond’s great smile waver and grow dim. His eyes filled so full that he had to take out his handkerchief and blow his nose loudly.

===Chinese===
再后来，他们看见他站在台上，尖着嗓子念他那首小小的爱国诗，一双眼睛亮得像星星，直勾勾地盯着台下听众里那张笑眯眯的宽脸膛。念完了，掌声把他臊得不行，一时竟忘了转身下台。他耷拉着脑袋，从眉毛底下偷偷往外瞧，朝观众露出一个古怪又害臊的微笑。贝西看见庞德先生脸上那个大大的笑容颤了颤，暗了下去。他眼眶涨得满满的，只好掏出手帕，大声擤起鼻子来。

===Original===
And they saw little ’Lias once more, for the last time. Mr. Pond’s buggy drove rapidly past their slow-moving hay-wagon, Mr. Pond holding the reins masterfully in one hand. Beside him, very close, sat ’Lias with his lap full of toys, oh, *full*﻿—like Christmas! In that fleeting glimpse they saw a toy train, a stuffed dog, a candy-box, a pile of picture-books, tops, paper-bags, and even the swinging crane of the big mechanical toy dredge that everybody said the storekeeper could never sell to anybody because it cost so much!

===Chinese===
最后，他们又见了小莱亚斯一面，那是最后一面。庞德先生的马车飞快地超过他们那辆慢吞吞的干草车，庞德先生一只手威风凛凛地攥着缰绳。他身边紧挨着坐着莱亚斯，怀里堆满了玩具，哦，满满的一怀——跟过圣诞节似的！在那惊鸿一瞥里，他们看见一列玩具火车、一只毛绒狗、一只糖果盒、一摞图画书、好几个陀螺、一些纸袋，甚至还有那台大机械玩具挖泥船上会摆动的吊臂——人人都说那玩意儿太贵，杂货店老板永远也别想卖给谁！

===Original===
As they passed swiftly, ’Lias looked out at them and waved his little hand flutteringly. His other hand was tightly clasped in Mr. Pond’s big one. He was smiling at them all. His eyes looked dazed and radiant. He turned his head as the buggy flashed by to call out, in a shrill, exulting little shout, “Goodbye! Goodbye! I’m going to live with﻿ ﻿…” They could hear no more. He was gone, only his little hand still waving at them over the back of the buggy seat.

===Chinese===
马车飞驰而过时，莱亚斯望着他们，小手轻轻地挥着。他的另一只手紧紧攥在庞德先生的大手掌里。他朝大家微笑着，眼神又恍惚又灿烂。马车掠过的一瞬，他回过头，发出一声尖细的、狂喜的呼喊：「再见！再见！我要去跟……一起住啦！」后面的话他们听不见了。他已经走远，只剩下那只小手，还在马车座背上一扬一扬地朝他们挥着。

===Original===
Betsy drew a long, long breath. She found that Ralph was looking at her. For a moment she couldn’t think what made him look so different. Then she saw that he was smiling. She had never seen him smile before. He smiled at her as though he were sure she would understand, and never said a word. Betsy looked forward again and saw the gleaming buggy vanishing over the hill in front of them. She smiled back at Ralph silently.

Not a thing had happened the way she had planned; no, not a single thing! But it seemed to her she had never been so happy in her life.

===Chinese===
贝西长长地、长长地舒了一口气。她发觉拉尔夫正在看她。有一会儿，她想不出他今天的样子为什么这么不一样。后来她看明白了：他在笑。她从来没见他笑过。他朝她微笑着，仿佛确信她什么都明白，一句话也没说。贝西又朝前望去，看见那辆亮闪闪的马车正在他们前面的山坡后消失。她也默默地朝拉尔夫笑了笑。

没有一件事像她计划的那样发生，一件也没有！可她觉得，她这辈子还从来没有这么快乐过。

## X Betsy Has a Birthday / 十　贝西过生日

===Original===
Betsy’s birthday was the ninth day of September, and the Necronsett Valley Fair is always held from the eighth to the twelfth. So it was decided that Betsy should celebrate her birthday by going up to Woodford, where the Fair was held. The Putneys weren’t going that year, but the people on the next farm, the Wendells, said they could make room in their surrey for the two little girls; for, of course, Molly was going, too. In fact, she said the Fair was held partly to celebrate her being six years old. This would happen on the seventeenth of October. Molly insisted that that was *plenty* close enough to the ninth of September to be celebrated then. This made Betsy feel like laughing out, but observing that the Putneys only looked at each other with the faintest possible quirk in the corners of their serious mouths, she understood that they were afraid that Molly’s feelings might be hurt if they laughed out loud. So Betsy tried to curve her young lips to the same kind and secret mirth.

===Chinese===
贝西的生日是九月九日，而内克罗塞特谷集市（Necronsett Valley Fair）年年都在八日到十二日之间举办。于是大家决定，贝西的生日就到举办集市的伍德福德去庆祝。那年帕特尼一家不打算去，但隔壁农场的温德尔一家说，他们的四轮篷车里可以腾出两个小姑娘的位子；因为莫莉当然也要去。其实照莫莉的说法，这集市办起来，有一半是为了庆贺她满六岁——那要等到十月十七日。可莫莉一口咬定，十月十七日跟九月九日挨得够近了，完全可以一并庆祝。这话让贝西直想放声大笑，可她瞧见帕特尼家的人只是彼此对望了一眼，一本正经的嘴角浮起一丝几乎看不见的笑意，便明白他们是怕大声笑出来伤了莫莉的心。于是贝西也试着把自己年轻的双唇，弯成同样一份好心的、心照不宣的笑意。

===Original===
And, I can’t tell you why, this effort not to hurt Molly’s feelings made her have a perfect spasm of love for Molly. She threw herself on her and gave her a great hug that tipped them both over on the couch on top of Shep, who stopped snoring with his great gurgling snort, wriggled out from under them, and stood with laughing eyes and wagging tail, looking at them as they rolled and giggled among the pillows.

===Chinese===
还有——我也说不清是为什么——这一番不肯伤害莫莉的心思，让她对莫莉涌起了一阵完完全全的热烈的爱。她扑到莫莉身上，紧紧搂住她，两个人一起翻倒在长沙发上，正压在谢普身上。谢普咕噜一声停了打鼾，从她们身子底下扭了出来，站在一边，眼睛带笑，尾巴直摇，看着她们俩在靠垫间滚作一团，咯咯直笑。

===Original===
“What dress are you going to wear to the Fair, Betsy?” asked Cousin Ann. “And we must decide about Molly’s, too.”

This stopped their rough-and-tumble fun in short order, and they applied themselves to the serious question of a toilet.

===Chinese===
「贝西，你打算穿哪条裙子去赶集？」安表姨问，「莫莉穿什么，咱们也得定下来。」

这一问立刻叫停了她们翻滚打闹的把戏，两人随即专心研究起梳妆打扮这个严肃问题来。

===Original===
When the great day arrived and the surrey drove away from the Wendells’ gate, Betsy was in a fresh pink-and-white gingham which she had helped Cousin Ann make, and plump Molly looked like something good to eat in a crisp white little dimity, one of Betsy’s old dresses, with a deep hem taken in to make it short enough for the little butterball. Because it was Betsy’s birthday, she sat on the front seat with Mr. Wendell, and part of the time, when there were not too many teams on the road, she drove, herself. Mrs. Wendell and her sister filled the back seat solidly full from side to side and made one continuous soft lap on which Molly happily perched, her eyes shining, her round cheeks red with joyful excitement. Betsy looked back at her several times and thought how very nice Molly looked. She had, of course, little idea how she herself looked, because the mirrors at Putney Farm were all small and high up, and anyhow they were so old and greenish that they made everybody look very queer-colored. You looked in them to see if your hair was smooth, and that was about all you could stand.

===Chinese===
这个大日子到来时，温德尔家的四轮篷车从院门口出发，贝西穿着一身挺括的粉白格子新布裙——那是她帮着安表姨一起做的——胖乎乎的莫莉穿着一件清爽的小白绉布裙，看着就叫人觉得好吃，那是贝西的一条旧裙子，折进一道深深的贴边才够短，正好配这个小团子。因为这天是贝西的生日，她坐在前排温德尔先生旁边；路上车子不多的时候，还不时由她来赶车。温德尔太太和她妹妹把后座挤得满满当当，从这头到那头，两人合成一面连绵不断的软和的怀，莫莉就快活地高坐在上面，眼睛发亮，圆圆的脸蛋兴奋得通红。贝西好几次回头看她，心想莫莉这模样真是好看极了。她自己长什么样，她当然没什么数，因为帕特尼农场的镜子又小又挂得高，何况都老得发绿，照谁都一副怪兮兮的颜色。照那些镜子只为看看头发梳没梳平，多看一眼都受不了。

===Original===
So it was a great surprise to Betsy later in the morning, as she and Molly wandered hand in hand through the wonders of Industrial Hall, to catch sight of Molly in a full-length mirror as clear as water. She was almost startled to see how faithfully reflected were the yellow of the little girl’s curls, the clear pink and white of her face, and the blue of her soft eyes. An older girl was reflected there also, near Molly, a dark-eyed, red-cheeked, sturdy little girl, standing very straight on two strong legs, holding her head high and free, her dark eyes looking out brightly from her tanned face. For an instant Betsy gazed into those clear eyes and then﻿ ﻿… why, gracious goodness! That was herself she was looking at! How changed she was! How very, very different she looked from the last time she had seen herself in a big mirror! She remembered it well﻿—out shopping with Aunt Frances in a department store, she had caught sight of a pale little girl, with a thin neck, and spindling legs half-hidden in the folds of Aunt Frances’s skirts. But she didn’t look even like the sister of this browned, muscular, upstanding child who held Molly’s hand so firmly.

===Chinese===
所以，当天上午过了一半，贝西和莫莉手拉着手在工业馆的种种奇观里穿行时，贝西在一面清亮如水的大穿衣镜里瞥见莫莉，真是大吃一惊。小姑娘鬈发的金黄、脸蛋的白里透红、那双温柔眼睛的蓝，都被照得那样真切，她都有些发怔。莫莉近旁还照出另一个女孩：黑眼睛、红脸膛、结结实实，两条结实的腿站得笔直，头高高扬起，无拘无束，黑眼睛从晒黑的脸上一亮一亮地望出来。贝西朝那双清澈的眼睛望了一会儿，然后……哎呀，天哪！她望的正是她自己！她变得多厉害呀！跟上一次在大镜子里看见的自己，差别有多大啊！那回她记得清清楚楚——跟着弗朗西丝姨妈在百货公司买东西，她瞥见一个脸色苍白的小姑娘，脖子细细的，两条麻秆似的腿半掩在弗朗西丝姨妈的裙裾里。可眼前这个棕色皮肤、结结实实、挺拔站立、紧紧牵着莫莉的手的孩子，跟那个小姑娘简直不像一个人，连姐妹都算不上。

===Original===
All this came into her mind and went out again in a moment, for Molly caught sight of a big doll in the next aisle and they hurried over to inspect her clothing. The mirror was forgotten in the many exciting sights and sounds and smells of their first county fair.

===Chinese===
这一切在心里一闪即逝，因为莫莉一眼看见隔壁过道里的大娃娃，两人赶忙跑过去查看人家的衣裳。镜子随即被忘到了脑后——她们的头一回县级集市上，好看、好听、好闻的东西实在太多了。

===Original===
The two little girls were to wander about as they pleased until noon, when they were to meet the Wendells in the shadow of Industrial Hall and eat their picnic lunch together. The two parties arrived together from different directions, having seen very different sides of the Fair. The children were full of the merry-go-rounds, the balloon-seller, the toy-venders, and the popcorn stands, while the Wendells exchanged views on the shortness of a hog’s legs, the dip in a cow’s back, and the thickness of a sheep’s wool. The Wendells, it seemed, had met some cousins they didn’t expect to see, who, not knowing about Betsy and Molly, had hoped that they might ride home with the Wendells.

===Chinese===
两个小姑娘可以随心所欲地逛到中午，然后到工业馆的荫凉底下跟温德尔一家会合，一起吃带来的野餐。两拨人从不同方向同时到齐，看到的是集市截然不同的两面。孩子们满脑子旋转木马、卖气球的、卖玩具的小贩和爆米花摊；温德尔夫妇交流的却是猪腿有多短、牛背塌了多少、羊毛有多厚这类见解。温德尔家看来还碰上了几位没想到会遇见的亲戚，那几家亲戚不知道贝西和莫莉的事，还指望能搭温德尔家的车回去呢。

===Original===
“Don’t you suppose,” Mrs. Wendell asked Betsy, “that you and Molly could go home with the Vaughans? They’re here in their big wagon. You could sit on the floor with the Vaughan children.”

Betsy and Molly thought this would be great fun, and agreed enthusiastically.

===Chinese===
「你看这样行不行，」温德尔太太问贝西，「你和莫莉跟沃恩家的一起回去？他们坐那辆大篷车来的。你们可以跟沃恩家的孩子一起坐在车厢底板上。」

贝西和莫莉觉得这一定很好玩，兴高采烈地答应了。

===Original===
“All right then,” said Mrs. Wendell. She called to a young man who stood inside the building, near an open window: “Oh, Frank, Will Vaughan is going to be in your booth this afternoon, isn’t he?”

“Yes, ma’am,” said the young man. “His turn is from two to four.”

“Well, you tell him, will you, that the two little girls who live at Putney Farm are going to go home with them. They can sit on the bottom of the wagon with the Vaughan young ones.”

“Yes, ma’am,” said the young man, with a noticeable lack of interest in how Betsy and Molly got home.

“Now, Betsy,” said Mrs. Wendell, “you go round to that booth at two and ask Will Vaughan what time they’re going to start and where their wagon is, and then you be sure not to keep them waiting a minute.”

“No, I won’t,” said Betsy. “I’ll be sure to be there on time.”

===Chinese===
「那好。」温德尔太太说。她朝屋里一个站在敞开的窗边的年轻人喊道：「喂，弗兰克，威尔·沃恩今天下午要在你那个摊位值班，是吧？」

「是的，太太。」年轻人说，「他值两点到四点。」

「那你告诉他一声好不好：住在帕特尼农场的两个小姑娘要搭他们家的车回去，她们可以和沃恩家的孩子们一起坐在车厢底板上。」

「好的，太太。」年轻人说，对贝西和莫莉怎么回家明显毫无兴趣。

「听好了，贝西，」温德尔太太说，「两点钟你到那个摊位去问威尔·沃恩，他们几点动身、车停在哪儿，然后你可千万别让人家等你一分钟。」

「不会的。」贝西说，「我一定准时到。」

===Original===
She and Molly still had twenty cents to spend out of the forty they had brought with them, twenty-five earned by berry-picking and fifteen a present from Uncle Henry. They now put their heads together to see how they could make the best possible use of their four nickels. Cousin Ann had put no restrictions whatever on them, saying they could buy any sort of truck or rubbish they could find, except the pink lemonade. She said she had been told the venders washed their glasses in that, and their hands, and for all she knew their faces. Betsy was for merry-go-rounds, but Molly yearned for a big red balloon; and while they were buying that a man came by with toy dogs, little brown dogs with curled-wire tails. He called out that they would bark when you pulled their tails, and seeing the little girls looking at him he pulled the tail of the one he held. It gave forth a fine loud yelp, just like Shep when his tail got stepped on. Betsy bought one, all done up neatly in a box tied with blue string. She thought it a great bargain to get a dog who would bark for five cents. (Later on, when they undid the string and opened the box, they found the dog had one leg broken off and wouldn’t make the faintest squeak when his tail was pulled; but that is the sort of thing you must expect to have happen to you at a county fair.)

===Chinese===
她们俩带来的四十美分里，还剩下二十美分可以花——其中二十五美分是采浆果挣的，十五美分是亨利姨父给的礼物。两人这会儿凑在一起盘算，怎么把手里这四个五分镍币花得最值。安表姨对她们毫无限制，说随便什么破烂玩意儿都可以买，只有粉色柠檬水不许喝。她说她听人讲，摊贩们在那水里又洗杯子又洗手，没准儿还洗脸。贝西一心想坐旋转木马，莫莉却眼馋一只大红气球；买气球的时候，旁边过来一个卖玩具狗的男人，小小一只只棕色的狗，尾巴是卷着的铁丝。他吆喝说一拉尾巴狗就叫；见小姑娘们望着他，他就拉了拉手里那只的尾巴。那狗发出一声响亮的尖叫，活像谢普的尾巴被人踩了一脚。贝西买下一只，整整齐齐装在盒子里，还系了根蓝细绳。五分钱买到一只会叫的狗，她觉得占了天大的便宜。（后来解开绳子打开盒子，她们发现那狗断了一条腿，怎么拉尾巴也发不出一丝声息；不过赶县级集市嘛，这种事你就得认了。）

===Original===
Now they had ten cents left and they decided to have a ride apiece on the merry-go-round. But, glancing up at the clock-face in the tower over Agricultural Hall, Betsy noticed it was half-past two and she decided to go first to the booth where Will Vaughan was to be and find out what time they would start for home. She found the booth with no difficulty, but William Vaughan was not in it. Nor was the young man she had seen before. There was a new one, a strange one, a careless, whistling young man, with very bright socks, very yellow shoes, and very striped cuffs. He said, in answer to Betsy’s inquiry: “Vaughan? Will Vaughan? Never heard the name,” and immediately went on whistling and looking up and down the aisle over the heads of the little girls, who stood gazing up at him with very wide, startled eyes. An older man leaned over from the next booth and said: “Will Vaughan? He from Hillsboro? Well, I heard somebody say those Hillsboro Vaughans had word one of their cows was awful sick, and they had to start right home that minute.”

===Chinese===
这时她们还剩十美分，便决定每人坐一回旋转木马。可是贝西一抬眼看见农业馆塔楼上的钟面，已经两点半了，她决定先去威尔·沃恩值班的摊位跑一趟，问问几点动身回家。摊位她一找就着，可里面没有威廉·沃恩，原先见过的那个年轻人也不在。换了个新来的，陌生的，一个吊儿郎当、吹着口哨的年轻人，袜子花哨极了，皮鞋黄极了，袖口条纹极多。听到贝西的询问，他说：「沃恩？威尔·沃恩？没听说过这个名字。」说完接着吹口哨，越过两个小姑娘的头顶朝过道两头张望。两个孩子瞪大了惊慌的眼睛仰头望着他。隔壁摊位一个上了年纪的男人探过身来说：「威尔·沃恩？希尔斯伯勒来的那个？唔，我听人说，希尔斯伯勒的沃恩家里捎来信儿，说他们家有头奶牛病得不轻，只好当场动身回家了。」

===Original===
Betsy came to herself out of her momentary daze and snatched Molly’s hand. “Hurry! quick! We must find the Wendells before they get away!” In her agitation (for she was really very much frightened) she forgot how easily terrified little Molly was. Her alarm instantly sent the child into a panic. “Oh, Betsy! Betsy! What will we do!” she gasped, as Betsy pulled her along the aisle and out of the door.

“Oh, the Wendells can’t be gone yet,” said Betsy reassuringly, though she was not at all sure she was telling the truth. She ran as fast as she could drag Molly’s fat legs, to the horse-shed where Mr. Wendell had tied his horses and left the surrey. The horse-shed was empty, quite empty.

===Chinese===
贝西从一时发懵中醒过来，一把抓住莫莉的手。「快！快！得赶在温德尔家走之前找到他们！」她心慌意乱（她实在吓坏了），忘了小莫莉多么容易吓着。她的惊慌霎时让孩子炸了窝。「哎呀，贝西！贝西！我们怎么办哪！」贝西拽着她跑过过道、冲出大门时，她喘着气直喊。

「哦，温德尔家不会已经走了的。」贝西安慰她说，尽管自己也不太确定这话是不是真的。她拽着莫莉那两条胖腿，能跑多快跑多快，直奔温德尔先生拴马、停四轮篷车的车棚。车棚空空的，空空如也。

===Original===
Betsy stopped short and stood still, her heart seeming to be up in her throat so that she could hardly breathe. After all, she was only ten that day, you must remember. Molly began to cry loudly, hiding her weeping face in Betsy’s dress. “What will we do, Betsy! What can we *do*!” she wailed.

Betsy did not answer. She did not know what they *would* do! They were eight miles from Putney Farm, far too much for Molly to walk, and anyhow neither of them knew the way. They had only ten cents left, and nothing to eat. And the only people they knew in all that throng of strangers had gone back to Hillsboro.

“What will we do, Betsy?” Molly kept on crying out, horrified by Betsy’s silence and evident consternation.

===Chinese===
贝西猛地站住，一动不动，心好像提到了嗓子眼，憋得她喘不上气。别忘了，她那天才刚刚十岁。莫莉放声大哭起来，把满是泪水的脸埋进贝西的裙子里。「我们怎么办哪，贝西！我们到底怎么办哪！」她哭喊道。

贝西没有回答。她也真不知道她们该怎么办！这里离帕特尼农场八英里，莫莉绝走不了那么远，再说她们俩谁也不认得路。她们只剩十美分，一点儿吃的也没有。满场陌生的人海里，她们认识的那些人，全回希尔斯伯勒去了。

「我们怎么办哪，贝西？」莫莉不停地哭喊，贝西的沉默和明摆着的惊慌失措把她吓坏了。

===Original===
The other child’s head swam. She tried again the formula which had helped her when Molly fell into the Wolf Pit, and asked herself, desperately, “What would Cousin Ann do if she were here!” But that did not help her much now, because she could not possibly imagine what Cousin Ann would do under such appalling circumstances. Yes, one thing Cousin Ann would be sure to do, of course; she would quiet Molly first of all.

At this thought Betsy sat down on the ground and took the panic-stricken little girl into her lap, wiping away the tears and saying, stoutly, “Now, Molly, stop crying this minute. I’ll take care of you, of course. I’ll get you home all right.”

===Chinese===
另一个孩子的脑袋嗡嗡直响。她又搬出莫莉掉进狼坑那回帮过她的老法子，绝望地问自己：「安表姨要是在这儿，她会怎么办？」可这回这法子不太管用了，因为在这样骇人的境地之下，安表姨会怎么办，她实在想象不出来。嗯，有一件事安表姨准会做，这是肯定的——她会头一个先把莫莉安抚住。

想到这里，贝西往地上一坐，把吓得魂不附体的小姑娘抱到膝上，替她抹去眼泪，硬起嗓子朗声说：「好了，莫莉，马上别哭了。我当然会照顾你。我一定平平安安带你回家。」

===Original===
“How’ll you ever do it?” sobbed Molly.

“Everybody’s gone and left us. We can’t walk!”

“Never you mind how,” said Betsy, trying to be facetious and mock-mysterious, though her own under lip was quivering a little. “That’s my surprise party for you. Just you wait. Now come on back to that booth. Maybe Will Vaughan didn’t go home with his folks.”

===Chinese===
「那你怎么带呀？」莫莉抽抽搭搭地问。

「大家都把我们丢下了。我们走不回去的！」

「你别管怎么带，」贝西说，拼命想显出俏皮又故弄玄虚的样子，尽管她自己的下嘴唇也有点儿在抖，「这是我给你准备的惊喜。你等着瞧好了。走，先回那个摊位去。说不定威尔·沃恩没跟他家里人一起回去呢。」

===Original===
She had very little hope of this, and only went back there because it seemed to her a little less dauntingly strange than every other spot in the howling wilderness about her; for all at once the Fair, which had seemed so lively and cheerful and gay before, seemed now a horrible, frightening, noisy place, full of hurried strangers who came and went their own ways, with not a glance out of their hard eyes for two little girls stranded far from home.

===Chinese===
这话她自己也几乎不信，回去只是因为在她看来，比起四周这片喧嚣的荒野里随便哪个地方，那儿总算没那么陌生吓人。因为忽然之间，方才还那么热闹欢快的集市，一下子变成了一个吓人的、闹哄哄的地方，满是行色匆匆的陌生人，各走各的路，那一张张冷淡的眼睛，绝不会朝两个困在远处回不了家的小姑娘望上一眼。

===Original===
The bright-colored young man was no better when they found him again. He stopped his whistling only long enough to say, “Nope, no Will Vaughan anywhere around these diggings yet.”

“We were going home with the Vaughans,” murmured Betsy, in a low tone, hoping for some help from him.

“Looks as though you’d better go home on the cars,” advised the young man casually. He smoothed his black hair back straighter than ever from his forehead and looked over their heads.

“How much does it cost to go to Hillsboro on the cars?” asked Betsy with a sinking heart.

“You’ll have to ask somebody else about that,” said the young man. “What I don’t know about this Rube state! I never was in it before.” He spoke as though he were very proud of the fact.

===Chinese===
她们再找到那个花里胡哨的年轻人时，情况也没有好转。他停下口哨，只来得及说一句：「没影儿，这左近哪儿都没有什么威尔·沃恩。」

「我们本来要搭沃恩家的车回家的。」贝西低声嗫嚅，指望他能帮点什么。

「看样子你们最好坐火车回去。」年轻人随口出主意道。他把黑头发从前额往回捋得比刚才更直，目光越过她们的头顶。

「坐火车到希尔斯伯勒要多少钱？」贝西问，心一路往下沉。

「这个你得问别人。」年轻人说，「这个土里土气的乡下州，我可是一概不知！我从前压根儿没来过。」说那口气，倒好像挺引以为豪。

===Original===
Betsy turned and went over to the older man who had told them about the Vaughans.

Molly trotted at her heels, quite comforted, now that Betsy was talking so competently to grownups. She did not hear what they said, nor try to. Now that Betsy’s voice sounded all right she had no more fears. Betsy would manage somehow. She heard Betsy’s voice again talking to the other man, but she was busy looking at an exhibit of beautiful jelly glasses, and paid no attention. Then Betsy led her away again out of doors, where everybody was walking back and forth under the bright September sky, blowing on horns, waving plumes of brilliant tissue-paper, tickling each other with peacock feathers, and eating popcorn and candy out of paper bags.

===Chinese===
贝西转过身，走向刚才说起沃恩家的那位上了年纪的男人。

莫莉小碎步跟在她脚后，已经全然安心——贝西这不正有板有眼地跟大人们打交道嘛。他们说什么，她没听见，也没打算听。只要贝西的声音听着正常，她就什么也不怕了。贝西总有办法。她又听见贝西在跟另一个男人说话，可她正忙着看一排漂亮的果子冻玻璃杯，没往心里去。随后贝西又领着她出了大门。门外，九月明亮的晴空下，人来人往，有的吹着喇叭，有的挥着颜色鲜艳的纸羽毛，有的拿孔雀毛互相胳肢，还有的从纸袋里掏爆米花和糖吃。

===Original===
That reminded Molly that they had ten cents yet. “Oh, Betsy,” she proposed, “let’s take a nickel of our money for some popcorn.”

She was startled by Betsy’s fierce sudden clutch at their little purse and by the quaver in her voice as she answered: “No, no, Molly. We’ve got to save every cent of that. I’ve found out it costs thirty cents for us both to go home to Hillsboro on the train. The last one goes at six o’clock.”

“We haven’t got but ten,” said Molly.

Betsy looked at her silently for a moment and then burst out, “I’ll earn the rest! I’ll earn it somehow! I’ll have to! There isn’t any other way!”

“All right,” said Molly quaintly, not seeing anything unusual in this. “You can, if you want to. I’ll wait for you here.”

“No, you won’t!” cried Betsy, who had quite enough of trying to meet people in a crowd. “No, you won’t! You just follow me every minute! I don’t want you out of my sight!”

===Chinese===
这倒提醒了莫莉，她们还有十美分呢。「哦，贝西，」她提议道，「咱们拿五分钱买爆米花吧。」

贝西猛地一把攥紧小钱袋，把她吓了一跳；回答时声音也发着颤：「不行，不行，莫莉。这钱一分也不能动了。我打听清楚了，咱们俩坐火车回希尔斯伯勒要三毛钱。末班车六点开。」

「咱们只有一毛呀。」莫莉说。

贝西默默看了她一会儿，猛地说：「缺的钱我去挣！总得想办法挣来！不挣不行！没有别的路了！」

「行啊。」莫莉乖乖地说，一点没觉得这有什么不寻常，「你想挣就挣去吧。我在这儿等你。」

「不行，不许等！」贝西喊道，在人堆里约地方碰头这种事她已经受够了，「不行，不许等！你一步不落地跟着我！别离开我的视线！」

===Original===
They began to move forward now, Betsy’s eyes wildly roving from one place to another. How *could* a little girl earn money at a county fair! She was horribly afraid to go up and speak to a stranger, and yet how else could she begin?

“Here, Molly, you wait here,” she said. “Don’t you budge till I come back.”

But alas! Molly had only a moment to wait that time, for the man who was selling lemonade answered Betsy’s shy question with a stare and a curt, “Lord, no! What could a young one like you do for me?”

===Chinese===
两人继续往前走，贝西的眼睛慌乱地四处乱转。一个小女孩在县级集市上可怎么挣钱啊！她怕极了走上前去跟生人开口，可不开口又从哪儿起头呢？

「莫莉，你在这儿等着，」她说，「我不回来你别挪窝。」

可惜呀！那一回莫莉只等了一小会儿，因为卖柠檬水的男人对着贝西怯生生的发问，只回瞪了一眼，没好气地说：「老天，不行！你这么点大的小孩能替我干什么？」

===Original===
The little girls wandered on, Molly calm and expectant, confident in Betsy; Betsy with a very dry mouth and a very gone feeling. They were passing by a big shed-like building now, where a large sign proclaimed that the Woodford Ladies’ Aid Society would serve a hot chicken dinner for thirty-five cents. Of course the sign was not accurate, for at half-past three, almost four, the chicken dinner had long ago been all eaten and in place of the diners was a group of weary women moving languidly about or standing saggingly by a great table piled with dirty dishes. Betsy paused here, meditated a moment, and went in rapidly so that her courage would not evaporate.

===Chinese===
小姑娘们接着往前逛，莫莉安安稳稳、满怀期待地信着贝西；贝西却口干舌燥，心里空落落的。这会儿她们正走过一座棚屋样的大屋子，一块大牌子上写着：伍德福德妇女援助会供应热鸡肉晚餐，每份三毛五。牌子当然写得不对了——都三点半、快四点了，鸡肉晚餐早就一抢而光，吃饭的人换成了一群疲惫的妇人，有气无力地走动着，或蔫蔫地倚在一张堆满脏盘子的大桌旁。贝西在门口站住，琢磨了一小会儿，随即快步走了进去——趁勇气还没蒸发掉。

===Original===
The woman with gray hair looked down at her a little impatiently and said, “Dinner’s all over.”

“I didn’t come for dinner,” said Betsy, swallowing hard. “I came to see if you wouldn’t hire me to wash your dishes. I’ll do them for twenty-five cents.”

The woman laughed, looked from little Betsy to the great pile of dishes, and said, turning away, “Mercy, child, if you washed from now till morning, you wouldn’t make a hole in what we’ve got to do.”

Betsy heard her say to the other women, “Some young one wanting more money for the sideshows.”

===Chinese===
灰头发的妇人有点不耐烦地低头看她，说：「晚餐全卖完了。」

「我不是来吃饭的。」贝西使劲咽了口唾沫说，「我是想问问，您能不能雇我洗碗。洗完只要两毛五。」

那妇人笑了，看看小小的贝西，又看看那一大摞盘子，转身走开时说：「天哪，孩子，你就是从现在洗到明天早上，也洗不完我们要洗的。」

贝西听见她跟别的妇人说：「又是个小毛孩，想挣了钱看杂耍去。」

===Original===
Now, now was the moment to remember what Cousin Ann would have done. She would certainly not have shaken all over with hurt feelings nor have allowed the tears to come stingingly to her eyes. So Betsy sternly made herself stop doing these things. And Cousin Ann wouldn’t have given way to the dreadful sinking feeling of utter discouragement, but would have gone right on to the next place. So, although Betsy felt like nothing so much as crooking her elbow over her face and crying as hard as she could cry, she stiffened her back, took Molly’s hand again, and stepped out, heartsick within but very steady (although rather pale) without.

===Chinese===
就是现在，现在正是该想起安表姨会怎么做的时候。安表姨绝不会因为伤了心就浑身发抖，也绝不会让眼泪火辣辣地涌上眼眶。于是贝西狠着心，逼自己别再发抖、别再流泪。安表姨也绝不会向那股彻底泄气的可怕下沉感屈服，她会径直走向下一个地方。所以，尽管贝西最想干的事莫过于把胳膊肘往脸上一架、放声大哭个痛快，她还是挺直了腰板，重新拉起莫莉的手，迈步走了出去——心里难受极了，外表却稳稳当当（只是脸色相当苍白）。

===Original===
She and Molly walked along in the crowd again, Molly laughing and pointing out the pranks and antics of the young people, who were feeling livelier than ever as the afternoon wore on. Betsy looked at them grimly with unseeing eyes. It was four o’clock. The last train for Hillsboro left in two hours and she was no nearer having the price of the tickets. She stopped for a moment to get her breath; for, although they were walking slowly, she kept feeling breathless and choked. It occurred to her that if ever a little girl had had a more horrible birthday she never heard of one!

===Chinese===
她们俩又在人群里往前走，莫莉笑着，指点着那些年轻人的胡闹把戏——下午越拖越晚，年轻人越玩越疯。贝西视而不见，绷着脸望着他们。四点了。回希尔斯伯勒的末班车还有两个钟头就开，可车票钱她还是一分也没挣到。她停下来喘了口气；虽然走得不快，她却总觉得气短、胸口堵得慌。她忽然想到，要是有哪个小姑娘过过比这更倒霉的生日，那她可从没听说过！

===Original===
“Oh, I wish I could, Dan!” said a young voice near her. “But honest! Momma’d just eat me up alive if I left the booth for a minute!”

Betsy turned quickly. A very pretty girl with yellow hair and blue eyes (she looked as Molly might when she was grown up) was leaning over the edge of a little canvas-covered booth, the sign of which announced that homemade doughnuts and soft drinks were for sale there. A young man, very flushed and gay, was pulling at the girl’s blue gingham sleeve. “Oh, come on, Annie. Just one turn! The floor’s elegant. You can keep an eye on the booth from the hall! Nobody’s going to run away with the old thing anyhow!”

“Honest, I’d love to! But I got a great lot of dishes to wash, too! You know Momma!” She looked longingly toward the open-air dancing floor, out from which just then floated a burst of brazen music.

===Chinese===
「哎呀，我真想去，丹！」她身旁一个年轻的声音说，「可是说真的！我要是离开摊位一分钟，妈妈能把我活活吃了！」

贝西飞快回头。一个非常漂亮的姑娘，黄头发蓝眼睛（活像莫莉长大以后的样子），正伏在一个小帆布棚摊的边上，招牌上写着此处出售自制的炸面圈和汽水。一个红光满面、兴致极高的年轻男人正拉着姑娘的蓝格子布袖子。「哎呀，来嘛，安妮。就转一圈！地板好得很呢。你从大厅这儿就看得住摊子！反正那老玩意儿也没人会偷！」

「说真的，我太想去了！可我也有一大堆盘子要洗！你又不是不知道我妈！」她朝露天的舞池望过去，满脸渴望，那边正好飘过来一阵喧腾的铜管乐。

===Original===
“Oh, *please*!” said a small voice. “I’ll do it for twenty cents.”

Betsy stood by the girl’s elbow, all quivering earnestness.

“Do what, kiddie?” asked the girl in a good-natured surprise.

“Everything!” said Betsy, compendiously. “Everything! Wash the dishes, tend the booth; *you* can go dance! I’ll do it for twenty cents.”

The eyes of the girl and the man met in high amusement. “My! Aren’t we up and coming!” said the man. “You’re most as big as a pint-cup, aren’t you?” he said to Betsy.

The little girl flushed﻿—she detested being laughed at﻿—but she looked straight into the laughing eyes. “I’m ten years old today,” she said, “and I can wash dishes as well as anybody.” She spoke with dignity.

===Chinese===
「哦，求求你！」一个小小的声音说，「两毛钱，我来干。」

贝西站在姑娘的胳膊肘旁，浑身上下都是急得发颤的认真。

「干什么呀，小家伙？」姑娘又惊又和气地问。

「什么都干！」贝西一字一顿地说，「什么都干！洗碗、看摊，你去跳舞！两毛钱，我全包了。」

姑娘和男人饶有兴味地对视了一眼。「嚯，瞧这小家伙来劲的！」男人说，「你差不多有一品脱杯子那么高了吧，嗯？」他对贝西说。

小姑娘脸红了——她最恨被人取笑——可她直直地望进那双含笑的眼睛。「我今天满十岁了，」她说，「洗碗我洗得跟谁都不差。」她说得端端正正。

===Original===
The young man burst out into a great laugh.

“Great kid, what!” he said to the girl, and then, “Say, Annie, why not? Your mother won’t be here for an hour. The kid can keep folks from walking off with the dope and﻿ ﻿…”

“I’ll do the dishes, too,” repeated Betsy, trying hard not to mind being laughed at, and keeping her eyes fixed steadily on the tickets to Hillsboro.

“Well, by gosh,” said the young man, laughing. “Here’s our chance, Annie, for fair! Come along!”

The girl laughed, too, out of high spirits. “Wouldn’t Momma be crazy!” she said hilariously. “But she’ll never know. Here, you cute kid, here’s my apron.” She took off her long apron and tied it around Betsy’s neck. “There’s the soap, there’s the table. You stack the dishes up on that counter.”

She was out of the little gate in the counter in a twinkling, just as Molly, in answer to a beckoning gesture from Betsy, came in. “Hello, there’s another one!” said the gay young man, gayer and gayer. “Hello, button! What you going to do? I suppose when they try to crack the safe you’ll run at them and bark and drive them away!”

Molly opened her sweet, blue eyes very wide, not understanding a single word. The girl laughed, swooped back, gave Molly a kiss, and disappeared, running side by side with the young man toward the dance hall.

===Chinese===
年轻男人放声大笑。

「好样的丫头，是不是！」他对姑娘说，接着又说，「哎，安妮，干吗不呢？你妈一个钟头内回不来。这丫头能看着货，不让人顺手牵羊，还有……」

「盘子也归我洗。」贝西又说了一遍，拼命不去在意别人的笑，眼睛稳稳地盯住那两张回希尔斯伯勒的车票。

「嘿，好家伙，」年轻男人大笑着说，「安妮，这回咱们的机会是真来了！走！」

姑娘也兴高采烈地笑了。「妈还不得疯了！」她乐呵呵地说，「不过她永远都不会知道。来，小可爱，这是我的围裙。」她解下长围裙，系到贝西脖子上。「肥皂在那儿，桌子在那儿。盘子你摞到那个台子上。」

她一眨眼就出了柜台的小门，这时莫莉正应着贝西的招呼走进来。「嘿，又来了一个！」那个快活的年轻人说，越来越快活，「你好啊，小扣子！你要干什么？等会儿有人来撬保险柜，你就冲他们汪汪叫，把他们轰跑，是不是？」

莫莉把她那双甜甜的蓝眼睛睁得溜圆，一个字也没听懂。姑娘大笑起来，一个箭步折回来，亲了莫莉一下，就不见了——跟年轻男人并肩朝舞厅跑去。

===Original===
Betsy mounted on a soap box and began joyfully to wash the dishes. She had never thought that ever in her life would she simply *love* to wash dishes beyond anything else! But it was so. Her relief was so great that she could have kissed the coarse, thick plates and glasses as she washed them.

“It’s all right, Molly; it’s all right!” she quavered exultantly to Molly over her shoulder. But as Molly had not (from the moment Betsy took command) suspected that it was not all right, she only nodded and asked if she might sit up on a barrel where she could watch the crowd go by.

“I guess you could. I don’t know why *not*,” said Betsy doubtfully. She lifted her up and went back to her dishes. Never were dishes washed better!

===Chinese===
贝西踩上一只肥皂箱，欢天喜地地洗起盘子来。她从来没想过，这辈子会有宁愿洗碗、别的什么都不想干的时候！可偏偏就是这样。她那口气松得太大，洗着那些粗糙厚实的盘子和杯子，简直想亲它们一口。

「没事了，莫莉；没事了！」她回过头，颤着嗓子欢天喜地地对莫莉说。可莫莉呢，打贝西一拿主意起就没觉得有什么事，只是点点头，问能不能坐到一只木桶上去，好看人从眼前走过。

「可以吧。也没什么不可以的。」贝西不大有把握地说。她把莫莉抱上去，回头接着洗她的盘子。天底下没有比这洗得更干净的盘子了！

===Original===
“Two doughnuts, please,” said a man’s voice behind her.

Oh, mercy, there was somebody come to buy! Whatever should she do? She came forward intending to say that the owner of the booth was away and she didn’t know anything about﻿ ﻿… but the man laid down a nickel, took two doughnuts, and turned away. Betsy gasped and looked at the homemade sign stuck into the big pan of doughnuts. Sure enough, it read “2 for 5.” She put the nickel up on a shelf and went back to her dishwashing. Selling things wasn’t so hard, she reflected.

===Chinese===
「请来两个炸面圈。」她背后响起一个男人的声音。

哎呀妈呀，有人来买东西了！这可怎么办？她走上前去，本想说摊主不在，她什么都不知道……可那男人放下一个五分镍币，拿了两只炸面圈，转身就走了。贝西倒吸一口气，看了看插在大面圈盘子里那张手写的价牌。果然，上面写着「五分两个」。她把镍币搁上架子，回去接着洗碗。卖东西也没那么难嘛，她想。

===Original===
As her hunted feeling of desperation relaxed she began to find some fun in her new situation, and when a woman with two little boys approached she came forward to wait on her, elated, important. “Two for five,” she said in a businesslike tone. The woman put down a dime, took up four doughnuts, divided them between her sons, and departed.

“My!” said Molly, looking admiringly at Betsy’s coolness over this transaction. Betsy went back to her dishes, stepping high.

===Chinese===
被追命似的那股慌劲儿一松，她开始在新处境里品出些趣味来。一个女人带着两个小男孩走过来时，她迎上前去张罗，又得意又郑重。「五分两个。」她用做买卖的口气说。女人放下一毛钱，拿起四只炸面圈，分给两个儿子，走了。

「嚯！」莫莉说，佩服地望着贝西做这桩买卖时的镇定劲儿。贝西回到盘子跟前，脚步抬得高高的。

===Original===
“Oh, Betsy, see! The pig! The big ox!” cried Molly now, looking from her coign of vantage down the wide, grass-grown lane between the booths.

Betsy craned her head around over her shoulder, continuing conscientiously to wash and wipe the dishes. The prize stock was being paraded around the Fair; the great prize ox, his shining horns tipped with blue rosettes; the prize cows, with wreaths around their necks; the prize horses, four or five of them as glossy as satin, curving their bright, strong necks and stepping as though on eggs, their manes and tails braided with bright ribbon; and then, “Oh, Betsy, *look* at the pig!” screamed Molly again﻿—the smaller animals, the sheep, the calves, the colts, and the pig, which waddled along with portly dignity.

Betsy looked as well as she could over her shoulder﻿ ﻿… and in years to come she can shut her eyes and see again in every detail that rustic procession under the golden, September light.

===Chinese===
「哦，贝西，快看！猪！大犍牛！」莫莉这会儿喊起来，从她的制高点沿着摊位间那条长草的宽巷子望过去。

贝西从肩膀上探过头去看，手上还不耽误认认真真地洗盘子擦盘子。获奖的牲口正绕着集市游行呢：得奖的大犍牛，亮闪闪的牛角尖上插着蓝缎花结；得奖的奶牛，脖子上套着花环；得奖的马，四五匹匹匹缎子般光亮，弯着明亮有力的脖子，蹄子落地轻得像踩在鸡蛋上，鬃毛和尾巴都编进了鲜艳的缎带；接着——「哦，贝西，快看那头猪！」莫莉又尖叫起来——是那些小些的牲口：绵羊呀，牛犊呀，马驹呀，还有那头猪，大摇大摆、体体面面地蹒跚而行。

贝西尽可能回过头去望……多年以后，她一闭上眼睛，仍能一五一十地重新看见九月金色光线下那支乡土气的队伍。

===Original===
But she looked anxiously at the clock. It was nearing five. Oh, suppose the girl forgot and danced too long!

“Two bottles of ginger ale and half a dozen doughnuts,” said a man with a woman and three children.

Betsy looked feverishly among the bottles ranged on the counter, selected two marked ginger ale, and glared at their corrugated tin stoppers. How *did* you get them open?

“Here’s your opener,” said the man, “if that’s what you’re looking for. Here, you get the glasses and I’ll open the bottles. We’re in kind of a hurry. Got to catch a train.”

Well, they were not the only people who had to catch a train, Betsy thought sadly. They drank in gulps and departed, cramming doughnuts into their mouths. Betsy wished ardently that the girl would come back. She was now almost sure that she had forgotten and would dance there till nightfall. But there, there she came, running along, as light-footed after an hour’s dancing as when she had left the booth.

===Chinese===
可她焦急地看了看钟。快五点了。唉，万一那姑娘忘了时间，跳起来没完可怎么办！

「两瓶姜汁汽水，半打炸面圈。」一个男人说，带着一个女人和三个孩子。

贝西急得冒火，在柜台上一排瓶子里翻找，挑出两瓶标着姜汁汽水的，瞪着上面带螺纹的铁瓶盖。这玩意儿到底怎么开呀？

「开瓶器在这儿，」男人说，「你要找的是这个吧。这样，你去拿杯子，我来开瓶。我们赶点儿时间，得去赶火车。」

唉，要赶火车的可不止他们一家啊，贝西悲苦地想。他们咕咚咕咚喝完，嘴里塞着炸面圈走了。贝西盼星星盼月亮似的盼着那姑娘回来。她几乎认定姑娘忘了这茬，要一直跳到天黑。可是，瞧，她来了，跑着来的，跳了一个钟头舞，脚步还像离摊时一样轻快。

===Original===
“Here you are, kid,” said the young man, producing a quarter. “We’ve had the time of our young lives, thanks to you.”

Betsy gave him back one of the nickels that remained to her, but he refused it.

“No, keep the change,” he said royally. “It was worth it.”

“Then I’ll buy two doughnuts with my extra nickel,” said Betsy.

“No, you won’t,” said the girl. “You’ll take all you want for nothing﻿ ﻿… Momma’ll never miss ’em. And what you sell here has got to be fresh every day. Here, hold out your hands, both of you.”

“Some people came and bought things,” said Betsy, happening to remember as she and Molly turned away. “The money is on that shelf.”

“Well, *now*!” said the girl, “if she didn’t take hold and sell things! Say﻿…”﻿—she ran after Betsy and gave her a hug﻿—“you smart young one, I wish’t I had a little sister just like you!”

===Chinese===
「给，小家伙。」年轻男人说着，掏出一枚两毛五的硬币。「托你的福，我们过了一辈子最痛快的时光。」

贝西把手里剩下的一个镍币递还给他，他摆手不要。

「不用，零头你留着。」他大手一挥地说，「值这个价。」

「那我用多出来的五分钱买两只炸面圈。」贝西说。

「不许，」姑娘说，「想要多少拿多少，不要钱……妈永远数不过来。再说这儿卖的东西每天都得是新鲜的。来，把手伸出来，你们俩。」

「刚才有人来买了东西，」贝西和莫莉转身要走时，恰好想起来，「钱在那边架子上。」

「嘿，瞧瞧！」姑娘说，「她还真上手卖起东西来了！我说……」——她追上贝西，搂了她一下——「你这个机灵的小家伙，真希望我有个一模一样的亲妹妹！」

===Original===
Molly and Betsy hurried along out of the gate into the main street of the town and down to the station. Molly was eating doughnuts as she went. They were both quite hungry by this time, but Betsy could not think of eating till she had those tickets in her hand.

She pushed her quarter and a nickel into the ticket-seller’s window and said “Hillsboro” in as confident a tone as she could; but when the precious bits of paper were pushed out at her and she actually held them, her knees shook under her and she had to go and sit down on the bench.

“My! Aren’t these doughnuts good?” said Molly. “I never in my life had *enough* doughnuts before!”

Betsy drew a long breath and began rather languidly to eat one herself; she felt, all of a sudden, very, very tired.

===Chinese===
莫莉和贝西急急忙忙出了大门，走上镇上的大街，直奔车站。莫莉一路走一路吃炸面圈。这会儿两人都很饿了，可贝西不把车票攥到手里，根本想不起吃东西。

她把两毛五加一个五分镍币塞进售票窗口，竭力用自信的口气说了声「希尔斯伯勒」；可等那几张宝贝纸片儿推出来、真真切切捏在手里时，她膝盖一软，只好过去在长椅上坐下。

「嚯，这炸面圈多好吃呀！」莫莉说，「我这辈子头一回炸面圈管够！」

贝西长出了一口气，也懒懒地拿起一只吃了起来；她忽然感到说不出的疲惫。

===Original===
She was tireder still when they got out of the train at Hillsboro Station and started wearily up the road toward Putney Farm. Two miles lay before them, two miles which they had often walked before, but never after such a day as now lay back of them. Molly dragged her feet as she walked and hung heavily on Betsy’s hand. Betsy plodded along, her head hanging, her eyes all gritty with fatigue and sleepiness. A light buggy spun round the turn of the road behind them, the single horse trotting fast as though the driver were in a hurry, the wheels rattling smartly on the hard road. The little girls drew out to one side and stood waiting till the road should be free again. When he saw them the driver pulled the horse back so quickly it stood almost straight up. He peered at them through the twilight and then with a loud shout sprang over the side of the buggy.

It was Uncle Henry﻿—oh, goody, it was Uncle Henry come to meet them! They wouldn’t have to walk any further!

===Chinese===
等她们在希尔斯伯勒站下了火车，拖着步子往帕特尼农场走的时候，她更累了。前面还有两英里路，这两英里她们从前也走过，可从来没有一天像今天这样漫长。莫莉一步一步往前蹭，沉沉地坠着贝西的手。贝西闷头赶路，脑袋耷拉着，眼睛又涩又困，像进了沙子。一辆轻便马车从她们身后的路弯上飞驰而来，独马快步小跑，像是赶车人有急事，车轮在硬路面上敲得脆响。两个小姑娘闪到路边站住，等车过去。赶车人看见她们，猛地一勒，那马几乎人立起来。他在暮色里端详了她们片刻，随即大喊一声，从车座边一跃而下。

是亨利姨父——哎呀，太好了，是亨利姨父来接她们了！再不用走路了！

===Original===
But what was the matter with Uncle Henry? He ran up to them, exclaiming, “Are ye all right? Are ye all right?” He stooped over and felt of them desperately as though he expected them to be broken somewhere. And Betsy could feel that his old hands were shaking, that he was trembling all over. When she said, “Why, yes, Uncle Henry, we’re all right. We came home on the cars,” Uncle Henry leaned up against the fence as though he couldn’t stand up. He took off his hat and wiped his forehead and he said﻿—it didn’t seem as though it could be Uncle Henry talking, he sounded so excited﻿—“Well, well﻿—well, by gosh! My! Well, by thunder! Now! And so here ye are! And you’re all right! *Well!*”

He couldn’t seem to stop exclaiming, and you can’t imagine anything stranger than an Uncle Henry who couldn’t stop exclaiming.

===Chinese===
可亨利姨父这是怎么了？他朝她们奔过来，连声喊：「你们没伤着吧？没伤着吧？」他弯下身，急切地上下摸她们，仿佛料想她们哪里摔断了。贝西感觉得到，他那双老手在抖，他整个人都在抖。等她说「咦，没事呀，亨利姨父，我们好好的。我们是坐火车回来的」，亨利姨父一下子靠在栅栏上，好像站都站不住了。他摘下帽子擦了擦额头，说——那简直不像亨利姨父在说话，他的声音激动成那样——「哎呀，哎呀——哎呀，我的天！好家伙！哎呀，老天爷！好了！你们到底在这儿！你们没事！哎呀！」

他似乎感叹个没完，而你简直想象不出比一个感叹个没完的亨利姨父更稀奇的事了。

===Original===
After they all got into the buggy he quieted down a little and said, “Thunderation! But we’ve had a scare! When the Wendells come back with their cousins early this afternoon, they said you were coming with the Vaughans. And then when you didn’t come and *didn’t* come, we telephoned to the Vaughans, and they said they hadn’t seen hide nor hair of ye, and didn’t even know you were *to* the Fair at all! I tell you, your Aunt Abigail and I had an awful turn! Ann and I hitched up quicker’n scat and she put right out with Prince up toward Woodford and I took Jessie down this way; thought maybe I’d get trace of ye somewhere here. Well, land!” He wiped his forehead again. “Wa’n’t I glad to see you standin’ there﻿ ﻿… get along, Jess! I want to get the news to Abigail soon as I can!”

“Now tell me what in thunder *did* happen to you!”

===Chinese===
等大家都上了车，他才安静了一点，说：「了不得！可把人吓坏了！今天下午温德尔家早早地带着亲戚回来，说你们搭沃恩家的车走。可你们左等不来、右等不来，我们给沃恩家打电话，他们说压根儿没见着你们的影子，压根儿不知道你们去了集市！告诉你吧，你阿比盖尔姨妈和我吓了个半死！我和安套上车，快得像一阵风，她赶着普林斯直奔伍德福德，我赶着杰茜走这边这条道，想着兴许能在哪儿寻着你们的踪迹。哎呀，乖乖！」他又擦了擦额头。「看见你们站在那儿，我可真高兴……驾，杰茜！我要赶紧把这信儿告诉你阿比盖尔姨妈！」

「现在跟我说说，你们到底出了什么事！」

===Original===
Betsy began at the beginning and told straight through, interrupted at first by indignant comments from Uncle Henry, who was outraged by the Wendells’ loose wearing of their responsibility for the children. But as she went on he quieted down to a closely attentive silence, interrupting only to keep Jess at her top speed.

Now that it was all safely over, Betsy thought her story quite an interesting one, and she omitted no detail, although she wondered once or twice if perhaps Uncle Henry were listening to her, he kept so still. “And so I bought the tickets and we got home,” she ended, adding, “Oh, Uncle Henry, you ought to have seen the prize pig! He was *too* funny!”

===Chinese===
贝西从头讲起，一口气讲到底。起初亨利姨父还愤愤地插话，为温德尔家这么不当心地撒手不管孩子而火冒三丈。可她越讲下去，他越安静，最后只留神地听着，一声不响，中途只插话催杰茜拿出最快的步子。

如今一切都平安过去，贝西觉得自己这段经历还挺精彩的，一个细节也没漏，只是有一两回她纳闷亨利姨父到底还在不在听，他安静得厉害。「就这样，我买了车票，我们到家了。」她讲完了，又添上一句，「哦，亨利姨父，你真该看看那头得奖的猪！太好笑了！」

===Original===
They turned into the Putney yard now and saw Aunt Abigail’s bulky form on the porch.

“Got ’em, Abby! All right! No harm done!” shouted Uncle Henry.

Aunt Abigail turned without a word and went back into the house. When the little girls dragged their weary legs in they found her quietly setting out some supper for them on the table, but she was wiping away with her apron the joyful tears which ran down her cheeks, such white cheeks! It seemed so strange to see rosy Aunt Abigail with a face like paper.

“Well, I’m glad to see ye,” she told them soberly. “Sit right down and have some hot milk. I had some all ready.”

===Chinese===
马车这时拐进帕特尼家的院子，看见门廊上阿比盖尔姨妈胖胖的身影。

「找到了，阿比！没事！一根汗毛没伤着！」亨利姨父大声喊。

阿比盖尔姨妈一句话没说，转身进屋去了。两个小姑娘拖着乏透了的腿走进屋，见她正静静地在桌上给她们摆晚饭，可她正用围裙擦着顺着脸颊往下淌的欢喜的泪——好白的脸颊呀！看见红光满面的阿比盖尔姨妈脸白得像张纸，真叫人觉得奇怪。

「嗯，见到你们我就放心了。」她端端正正地对她们说，「快坐下，喝点热牛奶。我都备好了。」

===Original===
The telephone rang, she went into the next room, and they heard her saying, in an unsteady voice: “All right, Ann. They’re here. Your father just brought them in. I haven’t had time to hear about what happened yet. But they’re all right. You’d better come home.”

“That’s your Cousin Ann telephoning from the Marshalls’.”

===Chinese===
电话铃响了，她走进隔壁房间，她们听见她用不稳的声音说：「好，安。她们到了。你爸刚把她们带回来。出了什么事我还没来得及听。可她们没事。你最好回家来。」

「那是你安表姨从马歇尔家打来的电话。」

===Original===
She herself went and sat down heavily, and when Uncle Henry came in a few minutes later she asked him in a rather weak voice for the ammonia bottle. He rushed for it, got her a fan and a drink of cold water, and hung over her anxiously till the color began to come back into her pale face. “I know just how you feel, Mother,” he said sympathetically. “When I saw ’em standin’ there by the roadside I felt as though somebody had hit me a clip right in the pit of the stomach.”

===Chinese===
她自己走过去，沉沉地坐了下来。几分钟后亨利姨父进来，她用相当虚弱的声音管他要阿摩尼亚药瓶。他飞奔去拿，又给她找来一把扇子和一杯凉水，焦灼地守在她身边，直到那苍白的脸上重新有了血色。「你那滋味我知道，妈。」他体谅地说，「我看见她们站在路边那会儿，只觉得有人朝我心口窝狠狠擂了一拳。」

===Original===
The little girls ate their supper in a tired daze, not paying any attention to what the grownups were saying, until rapid hoofs clicked on the stones outside and Cousin Ann came in quickly, her black eyes snapping.

“Now, for mercy’s sake, tell me what happened,” she said, adding hotly, “and if I don’t give that Maria Wendell a piece of my mind!”

===Chinese===
两个小姑娘恍恍惚惚、累得迷迷糊糊地吃着晚饭，大人们说什么全没听进耳朵，直到外头石子路上响起急促的蹄嗒声，安表姨快步走了进来，一双黑眼睛亮得逼人。

「老天在上，快告诉我到底怎么回事。」她说，又气冲冲地添了一句：「我非得好好说说那个玛丽亚·温德尔不可！」

===Original===
Uncle Henry broke in: “*I*’m going to tell what happened. *I want* to do it. You and Mother just listen, just sit right down and listen.” His voice was shaking with feeling, and as he went on and told of Betsy’s afternoon, her fright, her confusion, her forming the plan of coming home on the train and of earning the money for the tickets, he made, for once, no Putney pretense of casual coolness. His old eyes flashed fire as he talked.

===Chinese===
亨利姨父抢过话头：「这事让我来讲。我就是要讲。你跟妈只管听，坐好喽听着。」他的声音激动得发颤。他讲起贝西这个下午——她的惊慌、她的无措、她定下坐火车回家的主意、她为车票钱想办法挣钱的经过——这一次，他把帕特尼家那副满不在乎的从容劲儿全丢了。他讲着讲着，一双老眼睛直冒火。

===Original===
Betsy, watching him, felt her heart swell and beat fast in incredulous joy. Why, he was proud of her! She had done something to make the Putney cousins proud of her!

When Uncle Henry came to the part where she went on asking for employment after one and then another refusal, Cousin Ann reached out her long arms and quickly, almost roughly, gathered Betsy up on her lap, holding her close as she listened. Betsy had never before sat on Cousin Ann’s lap.

===Chinese===
贝西望着他，只觉得心口发胀，难以置信地欢快地狂跳。哎呀，他是在为她骄傲！她做了件事，让帕特尼家的表亲们为她骄傲了！

亨利姨父讲到她一次次吃闭门羹、却还是一家家问下去的那一段时，安表姨伸出两条长胳膊，又快又近乎粗鲁地把贝西一把捞到膝上，紧紧搂着，一边听。贝西从前可从来没坐上过安表姨的膝头。

===Original===
And when Uncle Henry finished﻿—he had not forgotten a single thing Betsy had told him﻿—and asked, “What do you think of *that* for a little girl ten years old today?” Cousin Ann opened the floodgates wide and burst out, “I think I never heard of a child’s doing a smarter, grittier thing﻿ ﻿… *and I don’t care if she does hear me say so*!”

It was a great, a momentous, an historic moment!

Betsy, enthroned on those strong knees, wondered if any little girl had ever had such a beautiful birthday.

===Chinese===
等亨利姨父讲完——贝西告诉他的事他一件没落下——问道：「一个今天刚满十岁的小姑娘，你们说这事怎么样？」安表姨彻底打开了闸门，冲口而出：「我看天底下再没有哪个孩子干过这么聪明、这么有骨气的事了……她听见我也罢！」

这是一个了不起的、重大的、具有历史意义的时刻！

贝西端坐在那结实的膝头宝座上，心想天下可曾有过哪个小姑娘，过过这样一个美好的生日。

## XI “Understood Aunt Frances” / 十一　理解弗朗西丝姨妈

===Original===
About a month, after Betsy’s birthday, one October day when the leaves were all red and yellow, two very momentous events occurred, and, in a manner of speaking, at the very same time. Betsy had noticed that her kitten Eleanor (she still thought of her as a kitten, although she was now a big, grown-up cat) spent very little time around the house. She came into the kitchen two or three times a day, mewing loudly for milk and food, but after eating very fast she always disappeared at once. Betsy missed the purring, contented ball of fur on her lap in the long evenings as she played checkers, or read aloud, or sewed, or played guessing games. She felt rather hurt, too, that Eleanor paid her so little attention, and several times she tried hard to make her stay, trailing in front of her a spool tied to a string or rolling a worsted ball across the floor. But Eleanor seemed to have lost all her taste for the things she had liked so much. Invariably, the moment the door was opened, she darted out and vanished.

===Chinese===
贝西生日过后约莫一个月，十月里的一天，满树的叶子红红黄黄，两件顶顶要紧的大事发生了，而且可以说，就发生在同一个当口。贝西早就发现，她的小猫埃莉诺（她一直当它是只小猫，虽然它如今已是只成年的大猫了）不大在家待着。它一天两三回进厨房，大声喵喵叫着要牛奶要吃的，可狼吞虎咽一吃完，转眼就没影了。漫长的晚上，贝西下跳棋、念书、做针线、玩猜谜的时候，膝上再没有那团呼噜呼噜、心满意足的毛球，怪想它的。埃莉诺这么不把她放在心上，她还有点伤心，好几回拼命想留住它：在它面前拖一个拴着线的线轴啦，把一团绒线球滚过地板啦。可埃莉诺似乎把从前那些心头好的兴致全丢了。门一开，它准是一溜烟蹿出去没了影。

===Original===
One afternoon Betsy ran out after her, determined to catch her and bring her back. When the cat found she was being followed, she bounded along in great leaps, constantly escaping from Betsy’s outstretched hand. They came thus to the horse-barn, into the open door of which Eleanor whisked like a little gray shadow, Betsy close behind. The cat flashed up the steep, ladder-like stairs that led to the hayloft. Betsy scrambled rapidly up, too. It was dark up there, compared to the gorgeous-colored October day outside, and for a moment she could not see Eleanor. Then she made her out, a dim little shape, picking her way over the hay, and she heard her talking. Yes, it was real talk, quite, quite different from the loud, imperious “*meow*!” with which Eleanor asked for her milk. This was the softest, prettiest kind of conversation, all little murmurs and chirps and singsongs. Why, Betsy could almost understand it! She *could* understand it enough to know that it was love-talk, and then, breaking into this, came a sudden series of shrill, little, needle-like cries that fairly filled the hayloft. Eleanor gave a bound forward and disappeared. Betsy, very much excited, scrambled and climbed up over the hay as fast as she could go.

===Chinese===
一天下午，贝西追了出去，打定主意要逮住它带回来。猫发现自己被跟踪，就大步大步地蹦着走，一次次从贝西伸出的手边逃脱。它们就这样一路到了马棚，埃莉诺像一道灰色的小影子，嗖地钻进敞开的棚门，贝西紧随其后。猫闪电般蹿上通往干草棚那架陡得像梯子的楼梯，贝西也手脚并用地飞快爬了上去。比起外面十月的斑斓秋色，上头昏暗得多，好一会儿她才认出埃莉诺：一个影影绰绰的小影子，正在干草上择路而行。她还听见它在说话。是的，是真正的说话，跟它要牛奶时那声又响又霸道的「喵——！」完全两样。这是最轻柔、最好听的一套话，全是低低的咕哝、啾啾和哼唱。哎呀，贝西差不多听得懂呢！她真听得出来，那是情话；接着，插进这套情话里，忽然响起一连串细细的、针一样尖的啼叫，霎时灌满了整个干草棚。埃莉诺朝前一蹿，不见了。贝西激动得不行，在干草上连滚带爬，能追多快追多快。

===Original===
It was all silent now﻿—the piercing, funny little squalls had stopped as suddenly as they began. On the top in a little nest lay Eleanor, purring so loudly you could hear her all over the big mow, and so proud and happy she could hardly contain herself. Her eyes glistened, she arched her back, rolled over and spread out her paws, disclosing to Betsy’s astounded, delighted eyes﻿—no, she wasn’t dreaming﻿—two dear little kittens, one all gray, just like its mother; one gray with a big bib on his chest.

===Chinese===
这会儿全安静了——那又尖又逗的小啼叫，来得突然，停得也突然。草堆顶上一个浅浅的窝里躺着埃莉诺，呼噜打得整个大草堆都听得见，骄傲和幸福得简直盛不下。它眼睛亮晶晶的，弓起背，打个滚，摊开爪子，向贝西又惊又喜的眼睛亮出来的——不，她不是在做梦——是两只可爱的小猫崽：一只纯灰，跟妈妈一个模子；一只灰的，胸口挂着一大片白围兜。

===Original===
Oh! How dear they were! How darling, and cuddly, and fuzzy! Betsy put her fingers very softly on the gray one’s head and thrilled to feel the warmth of the little living creature. “Oh, Eleanor!” she asked eagerly. “*Can I* pick one up?” She lifted the gray one gently and held it up to her cheek. The little thing nestled down in the warm hollow of her hand. She could feel its tiny, tiny little claws pricking softly into her palm. “Oh, you sweetness! You little, little baby-thing!” she said over and over in a whisper.

===Chinese===
哦！多亲呀！多可爱、多好抱、多毛茸茸呀！贝西把手指轻轻搭在纯灰那只的头上，一触到那小生命温热的身子，心里就是一阵颤。「哦，埃莉诺！」她急切地问，「我能抱一只起来吗？」她轻轻捧起那只纯灰的，贴到自己脸颊上。小东西顺势偎进她手心温热的窝。她觉出那小小的、小小的爪子，轻轻地扎着她的掌心。「哦，你这个小甜心！你这小小的、小小的小奶猫！」她一遍又一遍地悄声说。

===Original===
Eleanor did not stop purring, and she looked up with friendly, trusting eyes as her little mistress made the acquaintance of her children, but Betsy could feel somehow that Eleanor was anxious about her kitten, was afraid that, although the little girl meant everything that was kind, her great, clumsy, awkward human hands weren’t clever enough to hold a baby-cat the proper way. “I don’t blame you a bit, Eleanor,” said Betsy. “I should feel just so in your place. There! I won’t touch it again!” She laid the kitten down carefully by its mother. Eleanor at once began to wash its face very vigorously, knocking it over and over with her strong tongue. “My!” said Betsy, laughing. “You’d scratch my eyes out, if *I* were as rough as that!”

===Chinese===
埃莉诺的呼噜一直没停，小主人跟它的孩子们认识的时候，它抬起头，眼神友好又信任。可贝西不知怎么觉出来，埃莉诺在为小猫担心，怕这只小姑娘虽然一心都是好意，可她那双又大又笨、不利索的人手，不会按正路子抱猫娃娃。「一点儿也不怪你，埃莉诺，」贝西说，「我要是你，也这么想。好了！我不碰它了！」她把小猫轻轻放回妈妈身边。埃莉诺立刻起劲地给它洗脸，用那有力的舌头把它舔得翻来滚去。「嚯！」贝西笑着说，「我要是这么粗手粗脚，你可要挠瞎我的眼了！」

===Original===
Eleanor didn’t seem to hear. Or rather she seemed to hear something else. For she stopped short, her head lifted, her ears pricked up, listening very hard to some distant sound. Then Betsy heard it, too, somebody coming into the barn below, little, quick, uneven footsteps. It must be little Molly, tagging along, as she always did. What fun to show Molly the kittens!

===Chinese===
埃莉诺像是没听见。不如说，它听见了别的动静。它猛地停住，头一抬，耳朵一竖，凝神听着远处什么声音。跟着贝西也听见了：有人走进下面的马棚，小小碎步，急急的，深一步浅一步。准是小莫莉，像平常一样跟了来。把小猫给莫莉看，多带劲呀！

===Original===
“Betsy!” called Molly from below.

“Molly!” called Betsy from above. “Come up here quick! I’ve got something up here.”

There was a sound of scrambling, rapid feet on the rough stairs, and Molly’s yellow curls appeared, shining in the dusk. “I’ve got a﻿ ﻿…” she began, but Betsy did not let her finish.

“Come here, Molly, quick! *quick!*” she called, beckoning eagerly, as though the kittens might evaporate into thin air if Molly didn’t get there at once. Molly forgot what she was going to say, climbed madly up the steep pile of hay, and in a moment was lying flat on her stomach beside the little family in a spasm of delight that satisfied even Betsy and Eleanor, both of them convinced that these were the finest kittens the world had ever seen.

===Chinese===
「贝西！」莫莉在下面喊。

「莫莉！」贝西在上面喊，「快上来！我这儿有好东西。」

只听得粗糙的楼梯上一阵急促乱爬的声响，莫莉的黄鬈发露出来了，在昏暗里闪着光。「我有一个……」她起了个头，贝西没让她说完。

「过来，莫莉，快！快！」贝西一边喊，一边急切地招手，仿佛莫莉晚到一秒，小猫就会化成空气跑了。莫莉把要说的事忘了个干净，发疯似的爬上陡陡的草堆，转眼就肚皮贴地，趴在那小小一家的旁边，喜得忘形——这副欢喜劲儿连贝西和埃莉诺都挑不出毛病，她们俩本来就拿准了，这是开天辟地以来顶好的一对小猫。

===Original===
“See, there are two,” said Betsy. “You can have one for your very own. And I’ll let you choose. Which one do you like best?”

She was hoping that Molly would not take the little all-gray one, because she had fallen in love with that the minute she saw it.

“Oh, *this* one with the white on his breast,” said Molly, without a moment’s hesitation. “It’s *lots* the prettiest! Oh, Betsy! For my very own?”

===Chinese===
「看，有两只，」贝西说，「一只送给你，归你一个人。还让你挑。你最喜欢哪只？」

她心里盼着莫莉别挑那只纯灰的，因为她一见面就爱上那只了。

「哦，要这只胸口带白的！」莫莉一秒也不犹豫，「这只漂亮多啦！哦，贝西！真归我一个人？」

===Original===
Something white fell out of the folds of her skirt on the hay. “Oh, yes,” she said indifferently. “A letter for you. Miss Ann told me to bring it out here. She said she saw you streaking it for the barn.”

===Chinese===
一个白色的东西从她裙子的褶子里掉出来，落在干草上。「哦，对了，」她随口说，「你的信。安小姐让我给你捎出来，她说她看见你一溜烟直奔马棚来了。」

===Original===
It was a letter from Aunt Frances. Betsy opened it, one eye on Molly to see that she did not hug her new darling too tightly, and began to read it in the ray of dusty sunlight slanting in through a crack in the side of the barn. She could do this easily, because Aunt Frances always made her handwriting very large and round and clear, so that a little girl could read it without half trying.

And as she read, everything faded away from before her﻿ ﻿… the barn, Molly, the kittens﻿ ﻿… she saw nothing but the words on the page.

When she had read the letter through she got up quickly, oh ever so quickly! and went away down the stairs. Molly hardly noticed she had gone, so absorbing and delightful were the kittens.

===Chinese===
是弗朗西丝姨妈的信。贝西拆开信，一只眼睛还看着莫莉，防着她把新宝贝搂得太紧，就着从棚壁缝里斜射进来的一柱蒙尘的日光读了起来。她读得毫不费力，因为弗朗西丝姨妈写字向来又大又圆又清楚，小姑娘不用费半点劲就能认。

她读着读着，眼前的一切都褪去了……马棚、莫莉、小猫……她什么都看不见了，只看得见纸上的字。

信从头到尾读完，她霍地站了起来，快得很，快得很！下楼去了。莫莉几乎没发觉她走了——小猫实在太让人着迷了。

===Original===
Betsy went out of the dusky barn into the rich, October splendor and saw none of it. She went straight away from the house and the barn, straight up into the hill-pasture toward her favorite place beside the brook, the shady pool under the big maple-tree. At first she walked, but after a while she ran, faster and faster, as though she could not get there soon enough. Her head was down, and one arm was crooked over her face.﻿ ﻿…

And do you know, I’m not going to follow her up there, nor let you go. I’m afraid we would all cry if we saw what Betsy did under the big maple-tree. And the very reason she ran away so fast was so that she could be all by herself for a very hard hour, and fight it out, alone.

===Chinese===
贝西走出昏暗的马棚，走进十月的绚烂里，却什么也没看见。她径直离开屋子，离开马棚，径直上到山坡牧场，朝她最心爱的那块地方去——小溪边、大枫树底下那方荫凉的水潭。起初她走着，走了一阵就跑起来，越跑越快，仿佛怎么也到得不够快。她低着头，一条胳膊挡在脸上。……

你们知道吗，我不打算跟她上去，也不带你们去。要是看见贝西在大枫树底下做了什么，恐怕我们大家都要哭了。她跑得那么快，为的正是能一个人待着，硬撑过那艰难的一个钟头，一个人扛过去。

===Original===
So let us go back soberly to the orchard where the Putneys are, and wait till Betsy comes walking listlessly in, her eyes red and her cheeks pale. Cousin Ann was up in the top of a tree, a basket hung over her shoulder half full of striped red Northern Spies; Uncle Henry was on a ladder against another tree, filling a bag with the beautiful, shining, yellow-green Pound Sweets, and Aunt Abigail was moving around, picking up the parti-colored windfalls and putting them into barrels ready to go to the cider-mill.

Something about the way Betsy walked, and as she drew closer something about the expression of her face, and oh! as she began to speak, something about the tone of her voice, stopped all this cheerful activity as though a bomb had gone off in their midst.

“I’ve had a letter from Aunt Frances,” said Betsy, biting her lips, “and she says she’s coming to take me away, back to them, tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
咱们还是安安分分地回到帕特尼一家待的果园，等着贝西没精打采地走进来，眼睛红红的，脸蛋发白。安表姨正在一棵树顶上，肩上挎着半篮红条纹的北探苹果；亨利姨父靠在另一棵树上的梯子里，往袋子里装着黄绿色、亮闪闪的磅甜苹果；阿比盖尔姨妈在四下走动，捡起花花色色的落地果，装进准备送往苹果酒坊的木桶。

不知是贝西走路的模样，还是走近时她脸上的神情，哦！还有她开口时那嗓音里的什么东西，让这一片欢腾的忙碌戛然而止，仿佛他们当中炸响了一颗炸弹。

「弗朗西丝姨妈来信了，」贝西咬着嘴唇说，「她说明天就来接我走，回她那儿去。」

===Original===
There was a big silence; Cousin Ann stood, perfectly motionless up in her tree, staring down through the leaves at Betsy. Uncle Henry was turned around on his ladder, one hand on an apple as though it had frozen there, staring down at Betsy. Aunt Abigail leaned with both fat hands on her barrel, staring hard at Betsy. Betsy was staring down at her shoes, biting her lips and winking her eyes. The yellow, hazy October sun sank slowly down toward the rim of Hemlock Mountain, and sent long, golden shafts of light through the branches of the trees upon this group of people, all so silent, so motionless.

===Chinese===
好大的一阵沉默。安表姨在树顶上纹丝不动地站着，透过树叶朝下盯着贝西。亨利姨父在梯子上转过身来，一只手还搭在一只苹果上，像冻在了那儿，朝下盯着贝西。阿比盖尔姨妈两只胖手拄着木桶，目不转睛地盯着贝西。贝西则盯着自己的鞋，咬着嘴唇，直眨眼睛。十月昏黄的太阳朝铁杉山的山脊缓缓沉下去，长长的金色光柱穿过树枝，照在这群人身上——全都这样沉默，这样一动不动。

===Original===
Betsy was the first to speak, and I’m very proud of her for what she said. She said, loyally, “Dear Aunt Frances! She was always so sweet to me! She always tried so hard to take care of me!”

For that was what Betsy had found up by the brook under the big red maple-tree. She had found there a certainty that, whatever else she did, she must *not* hurt Aunt Frances’s feelings﻿—dear, gentle, sweet Aunt Frances, whose feelings were so easily hurt and who had given her so many years of such anxious care. Something up there had told her﻿—perhaps the quiet blue shadow of Windward Mountain creeping slowly over the pasture toward her, perhaps the silent glory of the great red and gold tree, perhaps the singing murmur of the little brook﻿—perhaps all of them together had told her that now had come a time when she must do more than what Cousin Ann would do﻿—when she must do what she herself knew was right. And that was to protect Aunt Frances from hurt.

===Chinese===
贝西头一个开了口，她说的话，我可为她骄傲。她忠心耿耿地说：「亲爱的弗朗西丝姨妈！她一向对我那么好！她总是那么尽心尽力地照顾我！」

因为这正是贝西在小溪边、大红枫树底下找到的答案。她在那儿找到了一个笃定：不管还做什么，绝不能伤了弗朗西丝姨妈的心——亲爱、温柔、和善的弗朗西丝姨妈，她的心那么容易受伤，她又给了自己那么多年提心吊胆的照料。那上头有什么东西告诉了她——也许是温德沃德山幽幽的蓝影，正悄悄漫过牧场朝她爬来；也许是那棵大红大金的大树无声的辉煌；也许是小溪潺潺的低唱——也许是一起告诉了她：如今到了一个关口，她得做得比「安表姨会怎么做」更多——她得做自己心里知道是对的事。那就是：护着弗朗西丝姨妈，不叫她受伤。

===Original===
When she spoke, out there in the orchard, she broke the spell of silence. Cousin Ann climbed hastily down from her tree, with her basket only partly filled. Uncle Henry got stiffly off his ladder, and Aunt Abigail advanced through the grass. And they all said the same thing﻿—“Let me see that letter.”

They read it there, looking over each other’s shoulders, with grave faces. Then, still silently, they all turned and went back into the house, leaving their forgotten bags and barrels and baskets out under the trees. When they found themselves in the kitchen﻿—“Well, it’s suppertime, anyhow,” said Cousin Ann hastily, as if ashamed of losing her composure, “or almost time. We might as well get it now.”

“I’m a-going out to milk,” said Uncle Henry gruffly, although it was not nearly his usual time. He took up the milk pails and marched out toward the barn, stepping heavily, his head hanging.

===Chinese===
她在果园里一开口，沉默的魔咒就破了。安表姨匆匆从树上爬下来，篮子都没装满。亨利姨父僵着身子下了梯子，阿比盖尔姨妈穿过草地迎上来。三个人说的是同一句话——「信给我看看。」

他们就在那儿，肩膀挨着肩膀凑在一处读信，脸色凝重。然后，仍旧一言不发，全都转身回屋去了，把忘在树下的袋子、木桶和篮子撂在了外头。进了厨房——「唉，反正到晚饭时候了，」安表姨忙忙地说，像是不好意思自己失了沉着，「差不多也到了。不如现在就张罗。」

「我去挤奶。」亨利姨父粗声粗气地说，虽然离他往常的时候还早得很。他拎起奶桶，朝马棚大步走去，脚步沉沉，脑袋耷拉着。

===Original===
Shep woke up with a snort and, getting off the couch, gamboled clumsily up to Betsy, wagging his tail and jumping up on her, ready for a frolic. That was almost too much for Betsy! To think that after tomorrow she would never see Shep again﻿—nor Eleanor! Nor the kittens! She choked as she bent over Shep and put her arms around his neck for a great hug. But she mustn’t cry, she mustn’t hurt Aunt Frances’s feelings, or show that she wasn’t glad to go back to her. That wouldn’t be fair, after all Aunt Frances had done for her!

===Chinese===
谢普哼了一声醒来，跳下长沙发，笨手笨脚地蹦到贝西跟前，摇着尾巴往她身上扑，闹着要玩。这贝西可有点受不住了！想想吧，过了明天，她就再也见不着谢普了——还有埃莉诺！还有小猫！她弯下身搂住谢普的脖子紧紧抱了一下，喉咙里直发堵。可她不能哭，不能伤了弗朗西丝姨妈的心，也不能露出不乐意回去的样子。弗朗西丝姨妈为她做了那么多，那样不公平！

===Original===
That night she lay awake after she and Molly had gone to bed and Molly was asleep. They had decided not to tell Molly until the last minute, so she had dropped off peacefully, as usual. But poor Betsy’s eyes were wide open. She saw a gleam of light under the door. It widened; the door opened. Aunt Abigail stood there, in her night cap, mountainous in her long white gown, a candle shining up into her serious old face.

“You awake, Betsy?” she whispered, seeing the child’s dark eyes gleaming at her over the covers. “I just﻿—I just thought I’d look in to see if you were all right.” She came to the edge of the bed and set the candle down on the little stand. Betsy reached her arms up longingly and the old woman stooped over her. Neither of them said a single word during the long embrace which followed. Then Aunt Abigail straightened up hastily, took her candle very quickly and softly, and heavily padded out of the room.

===Chinese===
那天夜里，她和莫莉上了床、莫莉睡熟之后，她还醒着。她们商量好了，不到最后一刻不告诉莫莉，所以莫莉像往常一样安安稳稳地睡沉了。可怜的贝西却睁着眼睛。她看见门缝下透出一线灯光。灯光宽了；门开了。阿比盖尔姨妈站在那儿，戴着睡帽，白色长睡袍裹着山一样的身子，一支蜡烛从下往上照亮她严肃的老脸。

「没睡呢，贝西？」她看见孩子的黑眼睛在被子上面亮亮地望着自己，便悄声问。「我只是——我只是想进来看看你睡得好不好。」她走到床边，把蜡烛搁在小几上。贝西渴望地张开双臂，老太太朝她俯下身来。接下来那长长的一抱，两个人谁也没有说一句话。然后阿比盖尔姨妈赶忙直起身，又快又轻地拿起蜡烛，迈着沉沉的步子出了屋。

===Original===
Betsy turned over and flung one arm over Molly﻿—no Molly, either, after tomorrow!

She gulped hard and stared up at the ceiling, dimly white in the starlight. A gleam of light shone under the door. It widened, and Uncle Henry stood there, a candle in his hand, peering into the room. “You awake, Betsy?” he said cautiously.

“Yes. I’m awake, Uncle Henry.”

===Chinese===
贝西翻过身，一条胳膊搭到莫莉身上——过了明天，连莫莉也没有了！

她使劲咽了口唾沫，瞪着星光下白蒙蒙的天花板。门缝下又透出一线灯光。灯光宽了，亨利姨父站在那儿，手里拿着蜡烛，往屋里张望。「没睡呢，贝西？」他小心地问。

「嗯，我醒着，亨利姨父。」

===Original===
The old man shuffled into the room. “I just got to thinking,” he said, hesitating, “that maybe you’d like to take my watch with you. It’s kind of handy to have a watch on the train. And I’d like real well for you to have it.”

He laid it down on the stand, his own cherished gold watch, that had been given him when he was twenty-one.

Betsy reached out and took his hard, gnarled old fist in a tight grip. “Oh, Uncle Henry!” she began, and could not go on.

“We’ll miss you, Betsy,” he said in an uncertain voice. “It’s been﻿ ﻿… it’s been real nice to have you here﻿ ﻿…”

And then he too snatched up his candle very quickly and almost ran out of the room.

===Chinese===
老人家趿着脚走进屋。「我就是忽然想起来，」他迟疑着说，「你兴许愿意把我的表带上。坐火车，有块表方便点儿。我是真心愿意你带着它。」

他把表放在小几上——他自己珍爱的金表，他二十一岁那年人家送给他的。

贝西伸出手，紧紧攥住他那硬茧累累的老拳头。「哦，亨利姨父！」她起了个头，就再也说不下去。

「我们会想你的，贝西。」他的声音发飘，「有你在这儿……有你在这儿，实在……实在好。」

说完他也飞快地抓起蜡烛，几乎是跑着出了屋。

===Original===
Betsy turned over on her back. “No crying, now!” she told herself fiercely. “No crying, now!” She clenched her hands together tightly and set her teeth.

Something moved in the room. Somebody leaned over her. It was Cousin Ann, who didn’t make a sound, not one, but who took Betsy in her strong arms and held her close and closer, till Betsy could feel the quick pulse of the other’s heart beating all through her own body. Then she was gone﻿—as silently as she came.

But somehow that great embrace had taken away all the burning tightness from Betsy’s eyes and heart. She was very, very tired, and soon after this she fell sound asleep, snuggled up close to Molly.

===Chinese===
贝西仰面翻回去。「不许哭！」她狠狠地对自己说，「不许哭！」她把两只手攥得紧紧的，咬紧牙关。

屋里有什么动了动。有人朝她俯下身来。是安表姨——一声没出，一声也没有，只是用两条结实的胳膊抱住贝西，越搂越紧，紧得贝西能觉出对方急促的心跳，一下一下传遍自己全身。然后她走了——来得无声，去得也无息。

可不知怎么，这大大的一抱，把贝西眼睛和心口那股火辣辣的紧，全给抱没了。她累极了，累极了，没多久就偎着莫莉，沉沉睡去。

===Original===
In the morning, nobody spoke of last night at all. Breakfast was prepared and eaten, and the team hitched up directly afterward. Betsy and Uncle Henry were to drive to the station together to meet Aunt Frances’s train. Betsy put on her new wine-colored cashmere that Cousin Ann had made her, with the soft white collar of delicate old embroidery that Aunt Abigail had given her out of one of the trunks in the attic.

She and Uncle Henry said very little as they drove to the village, and even less as they stood waiting together on the platform. Betsy slipped her hand into his and he held it tight as the train whistled in the distance and came slowly and laboriously puffing up to the station.

===Chinese===
到了早晨，谁也没提头天晚上的事。早饭做了，吃了，饭后立刻套车。贝西和亨利姨父要一起去火车站，接弗朗西丝姨妈的火车。贝西穿上安表姨给她做的那件新的酒红色羊绒呢裙，领子是阿比盖尔姨妈从阁楼一口旧箱子里翻出来的、绣工精致的柔软老白衬领。

去村里的路上，她和亨利姨父话很少；在站台上一块儿等车时，话说得更少。火车在远处鸣笛，喘着粗气慢吞吞、费劲巴力地爬进站来，贝西把手悄悄塞进他手里，他紧紧握住。

===Original===
Just one person got off at the little station, and that was Aunt Frances, looking ever so dressed up and citified, with a fluffy ostrich-feather boa and kid gloves and a white veil over her face and a big blue one floating from her gay-flowered velvet hat.
